**What changed.** Each `--screenshot` / `--screenshot-every` frame and the first frame F2 shows a view on now leave a frame note (`screenshot <file>`, `stats overlay opened`). A hitch they account for is classed `tooling`: the hitch log writes `"class":"tooling"` with the tool as its `cause`, where a screenshot used to read `elsewhere, present 92.5 ms`. The exit report counts these on a new line, `[perf] tooling hitches (screenshots, the F2 overlay; never gated): ...`, and leaves them out of `[perf] hitches:`, the perf line, the F2 counters, `--capture-on-hitch` and every `--max-hitches` gate. Debug builds now build egui's text stack (epaint, harfrust, skrifa, vello_cpu) optimized, so the first F2 press costs about 12 ms instead of about 110 ms. A game's own text at a new size gets faster the same way, and a cold debug build takes a little longer.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a tool reads the hitch log's `class`. It can now say `tooling` as well as `player` and `elsewhere`.

### F3 shows an Expression panel when the player has an expression feed (#525)
**What changed.** A run with `--expression-timeline` or `--expression-listen` now gets an **Expression** panel among the F3 panels (builds with `debug-hud`, the default). It shows the latest `expression()` state as a key/value table with its source and how long ago it arrived in game time, the last 12 states newest first with what changed in each, the timeline's playhead and next event, and the socket's open connections and the lines it dropped (malformed, oversize) and the states it dropped under a flood. A state shows in the panel on the frame it is delivered. F3 now has a panel to open in a scene that had none, when a feed is running. Nothing changes for a run without a feed.
**What to do.** Nothing. Press F3 while rehearsing a timeline or driving the socket to see what scripts hear without `RUST_LOG=expression=info`.

### F4 → Styles has a Blend slider for every style (#521)
**What changed.** Each style's section in F4 → Styles (and the player's F3 Styles panel) now opens with a **Blend** row: a 0–1 slider for the table's `blend` key (#369), how much of the style shows over the frame it read. It has the ↺ reset (back to 1) and the dot that marks a value differing from the scene, like every other row, and Copy TOML and Commit to scene write it back (at 1 the table says nothing of it). Scripts were already able to pick a look, with `set_look(name)` and `set_look(name, seconds)` for a fade (#356, #496), so nothing else changed.
**What to do.** Nothing. A scene's `blend` keys read as before.

### Tracy (and the scope recorder) see inside physics, scripts, animation, loading and the renderer (#1096)
**What changed.** About 120 `flint_core::scope!` lines now sit below
the frame loop's sixteen: physics (`physics::fixed_update`, its step and both
syncs, the character), scripts (each callback kind, each script's call by
file, the hot-reload poll, compiles), animation (each tier, IK), particles,
audio, the procgen queue and living props, terrain, loading
(`import_model`, `decode_image`, `load_texture`, `upload_model`,
`scene_transition`, each by file or asset name) and the renderer's inner
steps (extraction, culls, uniforms, each post effect and style part as
`encode_part`, pipeline builds by label, egui's run, tessellation and
upload). Loops carry one scope with a count, never one an entity. A
`&Path` is now a scope detail (`scope!("load_level", path)`), read only
while the scope records. A default release build still carries none of it.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### `flint edit`: Commit to scene no longer resets the rest of F4 (#516)
**What changed.** F4 → Styles → **Commit to scene** writes one style into the scene file, and the viewer's file watcher then reloads the scene. That reload used to take the whole `[post_process]` from the file, so a bloom, fog or SSAO value (or another style's settings) tuned in F4 and never written anywhere snapped back to the authored value without a word. The reload a commit causes now leaves the post-process on screen as it was; those other values are still not in the file, and the button's hover text says so. Any other reload, such as an edit of the file in a text editor, takes the file's `[post_process]` as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. To keep a tuned bloom or fog, write it into the scene's `[post_process]` yourself; Copy TOML and Commit to scene carry only the one style.

### `flint_core::scope!` marks a scope for Tracy and Flint's recorder; a `trace` feature (#1095)
**What changed.** The engine's profiler scopes are `flint_core::scope!("name")`
lines (ADR 0128), with an optional count or text detail
(`scope!("load_level", path)`). One line reaches Tracy under the player's
`profile` feature, as `profiling::scope!` did, and Flint's own scope recorder,
which every debug build has and a release build gets with flint-player's new
`trace` feature (`trace = ["flint-player/trace"]` in a game's binary). Nothing
records yet: the recorder's file and flags arrive with #1097. A default
release build carries none of it. In Tracy the engine's zones are now named
by their function alone (`tick`, `render`, `update_from_world`), not by its
module path.
**What to do.** Nothing. To have a game's own scopes reach the recorder too,
write `flint_core::scope!` where it wrote `profiling::scope!` or
`#[profiling::function]`; the old forms still reach Tracy.

### A `profiling` Cargo profile for sampling profilers (#1101)
**What changed.** The engine's root `Cargo.toml` has a `profiling` profile: release's code with line tables in every crate, dependencies included. `cargo build --profile profiling -p flint-player` writes `target/profiling/`, which Superluminal, samply, WPA and Visual Studio's collector name function by function and line by line, inlined frames included. A cold build of the player takes about 4 minutes and 2.7 GB. The book's Profiling guide has a new section, "When scopes aren't enough", that covers which tool to use, the commands on Windows and Linux, and how to find a hitch from the hitch log on a sampler's timeline.
**What to do.** Nothing. For the same build of a game binary, copy the three lines of `[profile.profiling]` into the game workspace's root `Cargo.toml`. Cargo reads profiles only from there.

### Terrain, skinned meshes and the ocean are lit like every other mesh (#738)
**What changed.** The lighting WGSL is written once (`lights.wgsl`, `brdf.wgsl`, `shadows.wgsl`, `direct_light.wgsl`, `tonemap.wgsl`, `pbr_surface.wgsl` in `crates/flint-render/src/`), not copied into six shaders, so three surfaces that had drifted now match the meshes. Skinned meshes take `[environment]` `diffuse_wrap`, `oren_nayar` and sheen, and a light's `source_radius`. Terrain takes those too, plus the normal offset on the sun's shadow (less acne on slopes) and the meshes' specular. The ocean's shadow softens under a sun with an `angular_size`, as other surfaces' shadows do. A scene that sets none of these levers renders as before, apart from terrain under a casting sun. Here that moved two stylize goldens (both terrain), the skinned characters in `phase4_runtime`, and the terrain in `rolling_meadow`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a scene's skinned characters or terrain were tuned to make up for the missing levers: look at them again. Rust that built a pipeline from `include_str!("shader.wgsl")` (none outside the engine) uses `wgsl_sources::SCENE` now.

### `tween(entity, "component.field", target, seconds[, easing])` eases any numeric field; `fade_anim_layer` takes an easing (#734)
**What changed.** Scripts can ease any number or list of numbers a component holds (a light's `intensity` or `color`, a material's `emissive`, a transform's `position`) on game time with one call, which fires `tween_done` with `#{ entity, path }` when it arrives; a write by anything else (a `set_field`, physics) takes over. `fade_anim_layer(id, index, weight, seconds, easing)` eases a layer's weight, written as the layer's new `fade_easing` key (linear when absent, as before). Every timed change now names one set of easings, `flint_core::tween::Easing` (`flint_player::Easing` is it), and rides one `Ramp` / `Tween`: style tweens, layer fades, crossfades, the wooze field (byte-identical) and Living Props' knobs. A style tween now lands exactly on its target.
**What to do.** Nothing. A script that lerps a field in `on_update` can call `tween` instead. A game whose own Rhai defines a `tween` function with four or five parameters shadows the engine's; rename it.

### A crowd of per-entity props keeps every prop's style, and a cap names what it leaves out (#740)
**What changed.** Pencil, watercolor and user styles with `inputs = ["mask"]` held 63, 15 and 15 per-entity variants a frame, and they and the ownership pass (#384) stopped at mask id 255: past that, props drew plain or were drawn over by a whole-frame style, mostly with no message. They now share ASCII's growing variant table, so every variant gets a row and every id an owner (300 props on `grid = "object"` all draw). A user style's prelude declares `flint_params` as a storage buffer (`flint_params.rows[i]` still reads row `i`) and the mask id slots as `flint_slots` at group 0 binding 2. A cap that still drops something (ASCII's 8 dissolve bands, 8 snap grids and 8 fonts, one paper image a frame) warns once naming the first entity past it, and `flint render --report json` lists them as `style_drops`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a user style's WGSL read `flint_params.slots` directly: call `flint_slot(id)` instead.

### `--max-hitches-of`'s failure names that class's worst hitch (#1130)
**What changed.** When `--max-hitches` or `--max-hitches-startup` fails a run that `--max-hitches-of player` or `elsewhere` narrowed, the failure line's `worst` and `causes:` now cover only that class. They used to cover every hitch, so a player gate could fail on an 18 ms `sync` frame and name a 48 ms `elsewhere` hitch, the class it was told to ignore, as the worst. The count was always right. The exit report's `[perf] hitches:` line is unchanged and still covers every class.
**What to do.** Nothing. A tool that parses the failure line for the worst hitch now gets the gated class's.

### Per-entity styles: echoes now veil, and woozy meshes are masked where they're drawn (#737)
**What changed.** A per-entity style (ASCII, pencil, a user style reading the mask) handles two more cases correctly. A living prop's echoes in front of a styled entity now unmask it by their alpha, as glass and particles already did (#290). Before, the style drew the echoes as part of the entity behind them, so leaves in front of a glyph wall became glyphs, ink outlines included. A styled mesh with `shallow_wooze` is now masked where it was drawn. Before, its mask stayed at the rest pose, leaving unstyled holes where the mesh had drifted off and styling the pixels it had drifted away from. Billboards were already right. Scenes with neither case render byte for byte as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. A styled scene with echoes in front of a styled entity, or with a styled woozy mesh, looks different. Check those shots, or re-bless their goldens if a game keeps any.

### A tweened per-entity ASCII `cell` no longer piles up GPU objects (#507)
**What changed.** Since #507 a per-entity ASCII variant with screen bounds
gets its own band of cells even at `footprint` 1, and that band's textures
were sized exactly to its grid. A script tweening the entity's `cell` (with
`set_style_param`) changed the grid every few frames, and each new size
remade both variant-cell textures and kept one more bind group over them for
the rest of the run (24 animated frames kept 4). The textures now only grow,
as the dissolve's do: a smaller grid, or a frame with none, keeps the size
they have. Nothing on screen changes.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### Property and node clips follow `animator.playing` like skinned ones; `flint_animation::layer_edit` is `animator::edit_layer` (#733)
**What changed.** Property, node and skinned clips now follow the `animator` component by one rule set (`flint_animation::animator::Animator::apply_to`), the one #732 gave skinned clips. So `stop_clip` now pauses a property clip (it kept playing), a node clip started by `autoplay = true` keeps playing (it stopped after one frame), a finished node one-shot replays when played again, and a clip played backwards starts at its end. Every kind writes back what plays: `playing` turns `false` when a one-shot ends and `true` when `autoplay` starts one. For Rust code: `player::PlaybackState` is gone (one `ClipPlaybackState` for every kind, without the legacy `layer_clip` / `layer_weight` / `layer_time` fields; read `layers[0]`), `AnimLayer::to_toml` is gone, and `layer_edit::edit_layer_table(comps, i, |table| ..)` is `animator::edit_layer(comps, i, |layer| layer.clip(..).weight(..))`; write animator fields with `animator::write(&mut comps).clip(..).playing(true)`.
**What to do.** Nothing in scenes or scripts. A player extension that called `layer_edit` or read `ClipPlaybackState::layer_*` moves to the calls above.

### A hitch is now at least 24 ms past the median, at any refresh rate: `FLINT_HITCH_FLOOR_MS` defaults to 24 (#1109)
**What changed.** The hitch detector's floor went from 8 ms to 24 ms. A frame must still be over twice the median of the frames before it as well. On a 60 Hz display nothing changes, because the shortest hitch is still three refreshes (50 ms). On faster displays the floor no longer lets two missed refreshes through. At 240 Hz a frame that stood three refreshes (12.5 ms against 4.2) was a hitch and is not now; the shortest is seven (29.2 ms). The F2 graph's count, the hitch log, `--perf`'s line, `?perf=1` and the `--max-hitches` gate all read the same rule, so on a high-refresh machine they count fewer hitches. Without vsync, a 60 fps game's shortest hitch goes from 34 ms to 41 ms.
**What to do.** Nothing. To keep the old count for a comparison, set `FLINT_HITCH_FLOOR_MS=8`.

### The swapchain acquire is its own phase, `acquire`, and counts as `elsewhere` (#1123)
**What changed.** The player used to time its wait for the next swapchain image as part of the `render` phase. A compositor stall, or a full swapchain waiting for the GPU, therefore read as the engine's own render work. That wait is now its own phase, `acquire`. Like `present`, it is outside the player's work, so a hitch whose excess is in it is classed `elsewhere`, and its cause line reads `elsewhere, acquire 515.6 ms`. `--perf`'s line, `?perf=1`'s readout and a frame capture's `cpu` records list `acquire` beside the other phases. The F2 graph draws it in the grey `elsewhere` band. Each `--hitch-log` line gains `acquire_ms` (beside `present_ms`, filled for `elsewhere` hitches) and `gpu_ms`, the GPU time read back in that frame (`null` while the GPU isn't timed). `render` now reads lower wherever the wait was large: under vsync, `FLINT_VSYNC=0`, or a GPU-heavy look.
**What to do.** Nothing. A tool that reads the hitch log by key sees two new keys, and one that compares `render` across engine versions should add `acquire` back in.

### A hitch's `cause` names its frame notes only when they explain it (#1124)
**What changed.** The cause in `--hitch-log`'s lines, the exit report's `[perf] hitches` line, `--perf`'s line and `?perf=1` used to name a hitch's frame notes whenever it had any. Now the notes are named as the cause only when their times add up to at least half of how far the frame ran over the median. That is the same rule a script callback is blamed by. Otherwise the line names the script, the phase or `elsewhere` first, and the notes follow it: `sync +9.1 ms (also: reload poll 1.2 ms)`. `physics steps` is never a cause now. Like `clock stall`, it is the long frame itself: the clock paying back game time. It is still listed in the log line's `notes`. The `causes:` tally in the report follows the same rule, so a run that tallied `physics steps` now tallies `render` or `elsewhere`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a tool of yours matches cause strings. If it does, expect the phase and the `(also: ..)` form.

### UI documents get `button`, `list` and `slider`, focus and `on_ui`; `.ui.toml` mistakes are errors (#731)
**What changed.** A `.ui.toml` has three controls the engine drives from the arrows, a pad, the mouse and touch (built-in `ui_up`/`ui_down`/`ui_left`/`ui_right`/`ui_accept`/`ui_back` actions; Escape left to the game), with `[styles.<class>.focus|pressed|disabled]` tables, and the loading script hears `on_ui(element_id, event)`. New: `ui_focus`, `ui_focused`, `ui_get_value`, `ui_set_slider`, `ui_select`, `ui_set_items`, `ui_set_enabled`, `ui_is_shown`, `scripts/lib/menu.rhai`. The loader is strict: an unknown type, anchor, key, parent, class or style property is a script error at `load_ui` (it returned -1) and `flint check`'s `bad-ui`. Siblings stack in file order, not by id. An `anchor` on a child places it in its parent, outside the stack; a centred text, or an auto-height root, now centres on its measured size. A hidden element hides its grandchildren too. `ui_set_value(entity, v)` is `set_fill`. `flint render --scripts` draws the HUD; `--ui-show`, `--ui-focus`.
**What to do.** Run `flint check`; fix what `bad-ui` names. Reorder elements written for alphabetical stacking. Rename `ui_set_value` to `set_fill`.

### The F2 overlay's last-hitch line now says what the hitch log says (#1128)
**What changed.** The line under the F2 frame-time graph used to work out a hitch's cause by itself, and it ignored frame notes. On the frame where a style's pipelines were built, it said `last 154 ms: script ui +156` while the hitch log said `style build ascii, 154.2 ms`. Now it is the hitch's time followed by the same `cause_line` the hitch log, the exit report and `--perf` print: `last 269 ms: style build ascii, 255.2 ms`, `last 117 ms: script stall.rhai:on_update 100.0 ms`, `last 30 ms: render +14.0 ms`, `last 13 ms: elsewhere, present 1.0 ms`. Times carry one decimal, as they do in the log. A slow `on_draw_ui` is now named as a script too, not as the `script ui` phase.
**What to do.** Nothing. If you compare an overlay screenshot against the old wording, update the expected line.

### Collision layers, masked `raycast`, `shape = "mesh"` / `"convex"`, and colliders that scale (#729)
**What changed.** A game names its layers in `flint.toml` (`[project.physics] layers = ["world", "player", "pickup"]`); `collider.layer` puts a collider on one and `collider.mask` lists what it meets, and two colliders touch, or a sensor fires, only when each one's mask holds the other's layer. `raycast(.., max_dist, mask)`, `raycast_2d(.., mask)` and `move_character(id, dx, dy, dz, mask)` take a layer's name or an array of names; `collision_layers()` lists them. The character controller meets what its collider's mask meets. `shape = "mesh"` is the entity's model's triangles (`collider.model` names another), `"convex"` their hull; a dynamic `mesh` is made convex. **A collider now takes its entity's world scale** (and its `offset` does too; a joint's `anchor` stays metres, unscaled); before, it was built unscaled with a warning.
**What to do.** A collider on a scaled entity whose `size` you wrote already scaled is now that much bigger: divide its `size` (and `offset`) by the scale. Colliders with no `layer` or `mask` behave as before.

### `flint frame replay --pass`: step a shadow cascade or a style pass a draw at a time (#1204)
**What changed.** A replay could stop only the scene pass after N draws. `--draws N` now steps whichever pass `--pass` names: `Shadow cascade 0` to `2`, `Spot shadow`, `Style mask` or `Style albedo` (`Scene` is still the default). Every other pass draws whole, so a cascade's casters can be stepped while the scene reads the other layers as captured. The report gains `pass` (the stepped pass's own images against the captured ones) and `pass_draws`, and `controls` now carries `pass` and `draws` where it had `scene_draws`. In `flint frame open` the draws slider and `[`/`]` follow the selected pass when it can be stepped. The frame description now lists the style mask's and albedo's draws. In Rust, `flint_render::DrawControls` swaps `scene_draws: Option<usize>` for `limit: Option<DrawLimit { pass: SteppedPass, draws }>`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a script reads `controls.scene_draws` from `frame replay --json` (read `controls.draws`), or Rust code builds a `DrawControls` (write `limit: Some(DrawLimit { pass: SteppedPass::Scene, draws: n })`).

### `play_clip` and `stop_clip` work on a skinned character after its first frame (#732)
**What changed.** A skinned entity's `animator.clip` and `animator.playing` were read only when the engine first saw it, so `play_clip` never changed a tracked character's clip, `stop_clip` never paused it, and a finished one-shot never replayed. Both fields are now live, with the rules node animation already used: a new `clip` cuts to its start and cancels a crossfade in flight (`play_clip` also clears `blend_target`), `playing = false` holds the current frame, `true` resumes, and turning a finished one-shot back on replays it. The engine now writes the fields back so they say what plays: `clip` becomes the clip a crossfade lands on, `playing` turns `false` when a one-shot ends, and `true` when `autoplay` starts a clip.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a script calls `play_clip` every frame on a non-looping clip (it now replays each time it ends) or writes `clip` expecting no effect on a skinned entity (it now cuts). To tell a one-shot has finished, read `get_field(me, "animator", "playing")`.

### Mouse look turns the same at any frame rate; `character_controller` gains `start_yaw` and six tuning fields (#728)
**What changed.** The character controller took mouse look and the jump press inside each fixed physics step. A 30 fps frame (two steps) turned twice as far, and a frame with no step (about half of all frames at 144 Hz) lost its motion and its jump. Now the player takes both once a frame (`PhysicsSystem::character_frame_input`, before the fixed loop), and `update_character` reads only held keys. New `character_controller` fields, defaults as before: `start_yaw` (degrees about +Y; 0 faces +Z, 90 faces +X), `look_sensitivity` (0.172 degrees per raw mouse unit, which was 0.003 rad), `pitch_limit` (80 degrees, was 1.4 rad), `sprint_multiplier` (1.6), `gravity_scale` (1, times the physics world's gravity, which was a fixed 9.81), `step_height` (0.5) and `snap_to_ground` (0.2).
**What to do.** An `--input-script` route with `look`, tuned at a frame step coarser than 1/60, now turns less: double its `look` amounts at 1/30 (the showcase trailer went from 450 to 900). A game that calls `update_character` itself should also call `character_frame_input` once a frame. A scene laid out along +Z to suit the old fixed facing can use `start_yaw` instead.

### `flint frame diff` counts draw kinds; render captures name their engine commit (#1208)
**What changed.** `flint frame diff`'s "Draws by entity" lines count an entity's draw kinds instead of listing every draw: `removed  body in Shadow cascade 0: 91 draws: skinned shadow`, or `3 draws (2 pbr, 1 unlit)` when there are several (was `1 draw (pbr)`, and a 91-part body's kind 91 times). The `--json` document is unchanged. `flint render --frame-capture` now writes `engine.commit` and `engine.dirty` when the scene's project is in a git checkout, as the player's captures do, and `flint frame replay` says whether it replays at the commit that captured the frame or across commits. In `flint frame open`, a replay of a player capture keeps the captured frame's CPU phases, scripts and history in the bottom strip, marked "as captured", instead of saying the capture came from `flint render`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a script of yours reads `frame diff`'s text: read its `--json` instead.

### `--capture-on-hitch` passes over startup hitches; `--capture-on-hitch-startup` takes them (#1207)
**What changed.** `--capture-on-hitch[=N]` (on `flint-player` and `flint play`) now counts only hitches at or after t = 2 s of game time, the same cutoff the exit report's `startup hitches` line, the hitch log's `startup` flag and `--max-hitches` use. Before, a game's first N hitches were nearly always its loading (script warm-up, physics catch-up, first pipeline compiles), so the captures held the load and not the hitch felt mid-game. The first startup hitch it passes over is said once on the console (`[capture] passed over a startup hitch at t=1.32 s ...`). The new `--capture-on-hitch-startup` (which needs `--capture-on-hitch`) takes those too. A game binary calls `PlayerApp::capture_startup_hitches(true)` after `capture_frames`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless you capture the load on purpose: then add `--capture-on-hitch-startup`.

### A game's own mixer buses in `flint.toml`; `set_bus_volume(name, v)`; `Bus::SFX` / `Bus::MUSIC` (#730)
**What changed.** Buses are data: `sfx` and `music` always, plus any a game declares in `flint.toml` as `[audio.buses.<name>]` with `parent` (another bus, or `master`) and `volume`. They mix the same on the desktop and in the browser's worklet. `audio_source.bus`, a script's `bus` option and the new script function `set_bus_volume("voice", 0.5)` take any declared name; an unknown one is warned about once (a sound plays on `sfx`). In Rust, `flint_audio::Bus` is now a bus number: `Bus::Sfx` / `Bus::Music` are deprecated constants for one release (use `Bus::SFX` / `Bus::MUSIC`), and `Bus::from_name` gives way to `ctx.audio.bus(name)` / `AudioEngine::bus`. `AudioEngine::manager_mut()` is gone: start clocked content with `AudioEngine::host(|manager| ..)`. `--music-volume` / `--sfx-volume` now scale the volume `flint.toml` starts those buses at.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless you called `manager_mut` (use `host`) or want more buses (declare them in `flint.toml`). Rename `Bus::Sfx` / `Bus::Music` before the next release.

### `flint playtest`, input-script `expect` / `wait`, and the script `test_*` functions (#714)
**What changed.** `flint playtest <scene> [--input-script f] [--exit-after T | --timeout T] [--strict] [--dump name] [--report out.json]` plays a scene with no window, GPU or sound device on a fixed clock. It writes one JSON report, the same bytes every run, and exits 1 on a failure: a failed `test_assert`, a `test_fail`, an input-script check that didn't hold, any script error, a failed transition or a timeout. Input scripts take `expect <entity> <condition>` and `wait <...> [within S]` lines (flint-query conditions, plus `entities where`, `persist`, `log` and `event`); `flint play --input-script` now exits 1 when one fails. Scripts get `test_assert(cond, msg)`, `test_fail(msg)`, `test_pass()` and `test_log(key, value)`, which `flint play` only logs. A query field may name a vector's axis (`transform.position.y`). `play_sound_at` no longer warns on every call when there's no sound device.
**What to do.** Nothing. To guard a level, write an input script that walks it with `wait` and `expect` lines and run `flint playtest` in your tests (book: Playtesting).

### `flint capture <shots.toml>`: trailer and review footage from a shot list (#722)
**What changed.** A new command captures a shot list as deterministic frame sequences: each `[[shot]]` names a scene, `seconds`, and optionally `fps`, `width`/`height`, a `look`, a `seed`, an `input` script and `[[shot.take]]`s. Each take is one stepped run of `flint play`, written to `captures/<list>/<shot>/<take>/frame_NNNN.png` beside the list with a `take.json`. `--verify` captures each take twice and names every frame that differs, in pixels and levels, exiting 1 when they differ. `--encode mp4` writes an H.264 file a take when ffmpeg is on `PATH`, and `--dry-run --json` prints the plan. This replaces the showcase film's `tools/showcase/capture.py` and `compare.py`, which are gone; `tools/showcase/film.toml` is now a shot list (`[capture]`, `[[shot]]`). `flint init`'s `.gitignore` now ignores `captures/`.
**What to do.** Nothing. To film a game, write a shot list (the book's Filming a Showcase page; `showcase_game/trailer.shots.toml` is an example) and add `captures/` to your `.gitignore`.

### Ask a running game for its world: `--inspect-listen`, `flint query --attach`, `--dump-world` (#713)
**What changed.** `flint play` and `flint-player` take `--inspect-listen 127.0.0.1:<port>`: a loopback socket that answers `flint query` strings and `flint entity show` against the live world, a JSON line for each line asked. `flint query "<query>" --attach <port>` and `flint entity show <name> --attach <port>` ask it and print exactly what they print for a scene file; `flint query --data` adds each entity's component values, for a file or a game. `--dump-world out.json --dump-world-at 2,5 [--dump-query "<query>"]` writes the world at game times as one JSON document (default time: `--exit-after`'s), and a run that never reaches a time exits 1. `flint query`'s JSON keys now come out in alphabetical order, as `flint entity show`'s always have.
**What to do.** Nothing. To see a scripted run's state, add `--dump-world` to it; to poke a game you're playing, start it with `--inspect-listen` and ask with `--attach`.

## 2026-11-16

### `flint frame replay`, and the frame debugger steps a frame draw by draw (#1089)
**What changed.** A frame capture can be drawn again from its replay inputs. `flint frame replay <capture>` loads the authored scene, lays the captured world over it by name, writes back the renderer's state and draws the frame, then says whether the final image and the scene pass are byte-identical with the capture's. `--draws N` stops the scene pass after N draws, `--hide <entity>` leaves an entity out of every pass, `--skip <pass>` switches a pass off, `--outline <entity>` marks an entity's pixels; the replay is written as a capture (`--out`), so `show`, `diff` and `open` read it. In `flint frame open`, R replays, a slider (or `[` `]`) steps the scene pass, H hides the selected entity, O outlines it, and the passes can be switched off. `flint render --frame-capture` now writes `replay/` too, as the player's captures do; a missing or changed asset is named. The renderer takes `FrameInputs::draw_controls` (`DrawControls`), off by default: every golden is byte-identical.
**What to do.** Nothing. A game binary that builds `FrameInputs` with a struct literal and no `..Default::default()` adds `draw_controls: None`.

### `flint gen` refuses a spec value its generator can't use; `--list-generators`, `--describe` (#718)
**What changed.** A spec's `[params]` are checked against its generator's schema at every depth before anything is generated (dry runs too): a wrong type, a number out of range, a string that isn't a choice or a colour, a missing required key now stops `flint gen` with exit 1, naming the key path (`ops[2].columns`) and what it allows. Ignored keys stay warnings. `flint validate` and `flint check` report the same for every spec a scene's models and textures name (`spec-param`), and the runtime warns once a spec. `flint gen --list-generators` and `--describe <generator>` (`--json`, `--markdown`) print what a spec may say. `flint gen` now accepts the `mock` generator, as the resolver always did. In Rust, `flint_procgen::lint_params` is `validate_params`, and `flint_procgen_ai::check_params`, `IssueKind` and its `ParamIssue` are gone: use `flint_procgen::{validate_params, ParamIssue}`.
**What to do.** Run `flint check` on your project; fix any `spec-param` error it names. Rename `lint_params` calls.

### `flint frame open`: the frame debugger's window (#1088)
**What changed.** `flint frame open <capture>` opens a capture (from `flint render --frame-capture` or the player's `--capture-frame-at`, Shift+F2 and `--capture-on-hitch`) in a window. The passes are a tree with their GPU times and why a skipped one didn't run. The selected pass's image has zoom, pan, a texel readout of the stored values, channel, exposure and depth controls, and an A/B wipe against the pass before or the finished frame. Its draws are a sortable, filterable table that follows an entity through every pass. The CPU phases, GPU passes and the player's frame history run along the bottom. Up and Down step through the frame. `--pass`, `--target`, `--entity` and `--wipe` pick what it opens on, and `--screenshot out.png` draws one frame of the window with no window opened (`--json` answers `{"written": [...]}`). `flint frame image` now goes through the same texel mapping as the window, with the same output.
**What to do.** Nothing. Book: guides/frame-debugger.md.

### The player says what a scene loaded in one line, its real controls in another, and `--log-format json` (#716)
**What changed.** `flint play` and `flint-player` write everything through one log on stderr, under targets `load`, `transition`, `perf`, `report`, `script`, `input`, `music` and `player`. Stdout now carries only `--list-gpus`' answer. Each file loaded is a `debug` line, counted into one summary per scene: `Loaded <scene>: 36 entities, 1 model, 15 scripts ... in 3684 ms; no warnings`. The fixed "WASD / Space / Shift" banner is gone. It is replaced by one `Controls:` line listing the actions the scene uses: those its input config names, those its scripts read, and the controller's walk when the scene has one. New flags: `--log-format text|json` (one object a line) and `--quiet` (warnings, `--perf` and the exit report). Engine load lines in `flint render` moved from stdout to stderr.
**What to do.** A tool that read the player's stdout reads stderr, or takes `--log-format json`. `RUST_LOG=load=debug` lists every file again. A game binary with its own subscriber adds `flint_player::logging::tally_layer()` for the summary's counts, or calls `flint_player::logging::init`.

### `flint edit --render` works for every file, and a flag an editor doesn't take is an error (#712)
**What changed.** The six editors (scene viewer and track editor, model, procgen, texture pipeline, terrain, particles) run in one host, `flint_viewer::host`. `--render out.png` now writes a still for procgen meshes, procgen textures, texture pipelines (their maps side by side), terrain, scenes and the track editor too, not only models and effects. `--width`, `--height`, `--no-grid`, `--distance`, `--yaw`, `--pitch`, `--target` and `--fov` mean the same in every editor with a 3D view (terrain and procgen meshes gained the camera flags). A flag the file's editor doesn't take, which used to be ignored or warned about, now stops with an error naming the editors it applies to; `--anim-time` and a model's or effect's `--style` need `--render`. In the previewers egui panels now blend in display space as the scene viewer's do (#428), so translucent panels read slightly darker. Model and effect stills are byte-identical to before.
**What to do.** Drop any flag a script passes to an editor that never took it. Code calling `flint_viewer::app::run` or `run_editor` needs no change; `app::viewer` / `app::track_editor` hand the viewer to `flint_viewer::host::run` or `render_still` instead.

### `flint frame show | diff | image`: read a frame capture from the command line (#1087)
**What changed.** A new top-level command reads the folder `flint render --frame-capture` writes, with no GPU, scene or schemas. `flint frame show <capture>` prints the summary: scene, adapter, size, GPU time by pass, draws, memory. Its views are `--passes`, `--draws [--pass P] [--entity E] [--sort tris] [--culled]`, `--targets` and `--hitch`. `flint frame diff <a> <b> [--out dir]` names the passes, entity draws, targets and pass images that changed between two captures. `flint frame image <capture> <pass> -o x.png` copies a pass's image out viewable: HDR tonemapped, depth to grey, ids coloured. Every view answers `--json`. A capture's manifest gains optional `scene`, `engine`, `written_by`, `captured_at`, `cpu` and `frame.game_time` fields. That is no version bump: older readers ignore them. A capture newer than the reader is refused, naming both versions.
**What to do.** Nothing. To debug a frame, capture it and run `flint frame show` on the folder.

### `flint asset generate|resolve|regenerate --max-cost`, one AI cost ledger, `.flint/prices.toml`, `flint asset job resume` (#721)
**What changed.** AI asset generation now goes through the cost meter `flint gen` uses: `--max-cost <USD>` refuses a Flux, Meshy or ElevenLabs call before it is sent if it could pass the ceiling (`resolve` reports `over_budget` and exits 1 with an asset left unresolved). Every paid call, asset or procgen or look, is appended to one ledger, `.flint/ai_costs.jsonl` at the project root; the old `.flint/procgen_costs.json` is still read, never written. `flint asset costs` and `flint gen --cost-report` print the same report, whose text heading is now "By spec or asset". Prices are data: a project's `.flint/prices.toml` overrides any built-in rate. A Meshy model is a job from submit (`flint asset job list`), and `flint asset job resume <id>` finishes one a run didn't. In Rust, `GenerationProvider::submit_job`/`poll_job`/`download_result` and `JobPollResult` are gone, and `create_provider` and `resolve_scene` take a `Calls`.
**What to do.** Set your Meshy plan's credit price in `.flint/prices.toml` (`[calls.meshy] unit = "task"`, `usd = ...`). Point any script reading `procgen_costs.json` at `ai_costs.jsonl`.

### `flint render --set post.<key>=v` replaces the per-effect flags; `--help` is grouped (#708)
**What changed.** `--set` sets one key as a scene writes it: `post.<key>=v` for any `[post_process]` key (`flint render --list-effects` lists them), or `<style>.<key>=v` for a style's table, leaving the style on or off as it was (`--style` turns it on). The 70 per-effect flags (`--exposure`, `--bloom-intensity`, `--fog-*`, `--kuwahara-*`, `--contour*`, `--ascii*`, `--tear*`, `--fxaa`...) still work for this release, hidden from `--help`, and each prints its new spelling: `--fog-density 0.05` is `--set post.fog_density=0.05 --set post.fog_enabled=true`. They go in the next release. `--ascii-cell`, `--ascii-levels`, `--ascii-footprint`, `--ascii-edge-depth`, `--ascii-edge-normal` and `--ascii-no-edges` now tune the ASCII style's table even when it's off, as `--set ascii.<key>` does. `flint render --help` is grouped by heading.
**What to do.** Change scripts, variant files and goldens that call `flint render` with the old flags to the printed spelling. A Rust host that builds `RenderArgs` reads its flags by group now (`args.camera.distance`, `args.clock.time`).

### The player captures frames: `--capture-frame-at`, `--capture-on-hitch`, Shift+F2 and `capture frame` (#1086)
**What changed.** `flint-player` and `flint play` write the frame debugger's capture from a running game. `--capture-frame-at <secs>` (repeatable) captures at a game time, `--capture-on-hitch[=N]` the frame after each of the first N hitches, Shift+F2 the next frame, and `<t> capture frame` in an `--input-script` the frame at that line. Each is a `<scene>-t<secs>.flintframe/` folder under `--capture-dir`, or `captures/` at the project's root. It holds `flint render --frame-capture`'s manifest and images, plus the HUD's `UI` pass, the frame history around the frame (and the hitch), and `replay/` (the world, the frame's inputs, bone palettes, particles, assets by hash). The writing happens on a thread, and the capturing frames aren't counted as hitches. The manifest gains `trigger`, `scene`, `engine`, `cpu`, `replay` and `notes` (version 1 still). Desktop only.
**What to do.** Add `/captures/` to your game's `.gitignore` (new projects have it): a capture holds renders of your game. The capture key is the player's `capture_frame` debug key: Shift+F2, unless your input config's `[debug_keys]` moves it, and a game action bound to Shift+F2 keeps the key.

### Debug keys: `[debug_keys]` in an input config, F1 and `--list-keys`, and a key the game binds is the game's (#717)
**What changed.** The player's debug keys are a table. An input config's `[debug_keys]` block moves an action (`stats = "F10"`, `living_pick_next = "Shift+F7"`), unbinds one (`= "none"`) or turns them all off (`enabled = false`). A key any of the game's actions binds now goes to the game only: binding F5 to a quicksave or backquote to a console no longer also fires the debug key. A debug key's press never reaches the game's input either (a game binary's `app.input.is_key_down(KeyCode::F2)` no longer sees it). F5-F9, backquote and backslash act only in a scene with living props or a charted music session; elsewhere they are ordinary keys, and backquote, backslash and F9 no longer put up a "no chart is playing" note. F1 shows the table over the game, and `flint play <scene> --list-keys [--json]` prints it. The start-up banner names F1 instead of four keys.
**What to do.** A shipped game that wants no debug keys adds `[debug_keys] enabled = false` to its `config/input.toml`. A game binary that read a debug key from `app.input` binds it to an action instead, which makes it the game's. Otherwise nothing.

### `flint gen x.terrain.toml` bakes a terrain spec with no window, and the editor's heightmap export is fixed (#719)
**What changed.** `flint gen` takes a `.terrain.toml` and writes its 16-bit heightmap and RGBA splat map, the same bytes every run for a seed (`-o terrain/meadow.png` puts `meadow_splat.png` beside it; `-o terrain/` names both after `[meta] name`). It takes `-o`, `--seed`, `--strict`, `--dry-run` and `--json`, and warns about any spec key nothing reads, naming the key it probably meant. The terrain editor's Ctrl+E export used to write heightmaps with every 16-bit sample byte-swapped, so they read back as static. It now writes what `flint gen` writes. Baking a spec is also about 40 times faster, in the editor too, with the same output.
**What to do.** Re-export any heightmap you saved from `flint edit x.terrain.toml`, or run `flint gen x.terrain.toml -o <dir>/`. Fix the keys the new warnings name.

### A texture pipeline op with a bad key is an error naming `ops[i].key`, and every op key has help (#720)
**What changed.** Each `[[params.ops]]` op of a `texture_v1` pipeline is now read through one descriptor that holds its defaults, ranges and help. A value that used to fall back to the default silently is now an error that stops generation: a wrong type (`target = 3`, `columns = "x"`), a choice that isn't one of the op's (`mode = "addd"`), or a number outside the op's range (`columns = 0`, `octaves = 17`). `color_ramp` with `stops = []` is an error now, where it panicked before. `edge_erode` takes `noise_type = "voronoi"`, which it always ran but its schema left out. `cell_color`'s schema default now reads `#735947`, the nearest hex to the colour it applies; it used to say `#735A47`. A valid spec renders byte for byte as before. Every op key has a help line in the node editor's tooltips and in the flint-procgen README's new op catalog.
**What to do.** Run `flint gen` on your pipeline specs. If one fails, fix the key the error names.

### Depth of field under `chromatic_aberration` no longer rings bright surfaces in green (#1156)
**What changed.** While `chromatic_aberration` was above 0.001, the composite took green from the picture after `dof_strength` and `radial_blur` had blurred it, but read red and blue from the sharp source. Wherever a blur spread a bright surface, green spread and red and blue didn't, so emissive edges, flames and hard-lit edges out of focus got a green halo. An aberration far too faint to see caused it: a script that eases `set_chromatic_aberration` down by `exp(-k t)` and stops calling it leaves a small value behind, and the value stays. Red and blue now go through the same blurs at their offsets. Frames with depth of field and radial blur both off, or with no aberration, are byte-identical. A frame with both on costs the depth-of-field gather three times.
**What to do.** Nothing. If you dropped `set_dof` because of the ring, you can put it back. A script that eases the aberration out should end with `set_chromatic_aberration(0.0)`, so it doesn't pay for the extra gathers.

### `flint env` lists every `FLINT_*` switch, and a bad value warns (#715)
**What changed.** Every `FLINT_*` environment variable the engine reads is now in one table (`flint_core::env`). `flint env [--json] [--group g] [--set]` prints each with what it accepts, what unset means and its value in this shell, and exits 1 when one holds a value it can't take. `flint render`, `flint play` and `flint-player --help` end with the switches they read, and the book has a page of them (CLI Reference, Environment Switches). A value a switch doesn't accept (`FLINT_VSYNC=fasle`, `FLINT_FRAME_LATENCY=5`, `FLINT_LOD_SCALE=0`) used to be ignored or clamped in silence; it now warns once, naming the forms it takes, and the default is used. `FLINT_GPU_LABELS` with an unknown word, once the per-draw level, is now a warning and the default.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a script sets a switch to a value outside its range: run `flint env` to see. Engine code reads a switch through its getter (`flint_core::env::VSYNC.choice()`), never `std::env::var`.

### `flint render --frame-capture <dir>`: every pass's output image, beside the frame's description (#1085)
**What changed.** `flint render` can capture the frame it drew into a folder: `manifest.json` (the `--frame-dump` description, each pass now listing its `images`, written last) and `targets/NN-<target>.<ext>`, every target each pass wrote as that pass left it: the shadow cascades, the HDR scene before post, SSAO, each bloom step, the style mask, each style's output and its own targets, a user style's included. 8-bit targets are PNG, float targets OpenEXR half, depth OpenEXR f32. The last `surface` image is the `-o` output byte for byte. In Rust, `SceneRenderer::capture_next_frame` / `take_frame_capture` and `flint_render::frame_capture::write_capture` do the same for any host. The frame's textures are now made with `COPY_SRC`; a frame nobody captures draws the same bytes.
**What to do.** Nothing. To see what a pass drew, `flint render <scene> --frame-capture frame.flintframe` and open its `targets/`.

### `flint render --frame-dump <file.json>`: the frame's passes, draws, targets and timings as data (#1084)
**What changed.** `flint render` can write the frame it drew as JSON: every pass in the order recorded (named as `--gpu-time`'s rows), whether it ran and why not, the targets it read and wrote, its GPU time in that frame, and each draw by entity name, with the culled ones and their reason (frustum, lod, draw distance). Then the frame's settings, targets, lights and `RenderStats`. A `--time` span describes its last frame, and `--report json` gains `frame_dump`. In Rust, `SceneRenderer::describe_next_frame` / `describe_frame` and `flint_render::frame_capture::FrameDescription` (`"format": "flintframe"`, `"version": 1`) do the same for any host, and `RenderStats` is now `serde`. A frame nobody describes draws as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. To ask why a scene makes the draw calls it does, `flint render <scene> --frame-dump frame.json` and read the `Scene` pass's `draws`.

### Every `flint` command takes `--json`, and failures exit 1 (#710)
**What changed.** `--json` is now a global flag: every command that opens no window prints exactly one JSON document on stdout and sends its other lines to stderr. A failure exits 1, with the command's report (`"valid": false`) or `{"error": {"message", "causes"}}` still on stdout; a command-line mistake is an error document with exit 2. Window commands (`play`, `edit`, `prefab view`, the music windows) refuse `--json`. One command that exited 0 on a failure now exits 1: `asset resolve --strategy ai_generate|human_task|ai_then_human` now exits 1 when a reference could be neither generated nor handed to a person. `--format json` (validate, query, entity, schema) and `render --report json` still work and are what `--json` prints. `gen --report json` still prints a JSON line a variant; under `--json` they are one document's `variants`. Some progress lines that were on stdout are now on stderr (`asset resolve`'s batch lines, `Loaded skybox`, `Warning: No schemas directories found`).
**What to do.** A script that parsed a command's text can read its `--json` document instead. A script that read a progress line from stdout reads stderr.

### `flint play` takes every `flint-player` flag (#705)
**What changed.** `flint play` and `flint-player` share one set of flags,
`flint_player::PlayerArgs`, and one launch, `flint_player::launch`. Their
`--help` now differs only by `flint-player`'s `--bundle`. `flint play` gains
`--msaa`, `--quality`, `--render-scale`, `--look`, `--seed`, `--frame-step`,
`--screenshot`, `--screenshot-at`, `--screenshot-every`, `--exit-after`,
`--input-script`, `--stats`, `--perf`, `--compare`, `--split`,
`--pixels-per-point`, `--procgen-defer`, `--touch-controls`, `--safe-area`
and `--touch-device`. A scene plays the same under either command: on the same
scene and flags the two wrote byte-identical screenshots. A `--style` word with
bad syntax is refused when the flag is parsed, on both. Before, only
`flint play` checked it that early. A game binary that embeds the player can
flatten `PlayerArgs` into its own clap command and call `launch`, or call
`PlayerApp::step_frames(step, seed)` for `--frame-step`'s seeding rule.
**What to do.** Nothing. A capture script that called `flint-player` only to
reach these flags can call `flint play` now.

### The music commands are `flint music ...` now (#709)
**What changed.** The six rhythm tools moved under one command: `validate-suite` is `flint music validate`, `play-suite` is `music play`, `render-suite` is `music render`, `calibrate` is `music calibrate`, `play-chart` is `music chart play` and `replay-chart` is `music chart replay`. Their flags are unchanged. The old names still run for one release, each printing its new spelling on stderr, and are no longer listed in `flint --help`; `render-suite <x>.goldens.toml` still runs `flint goldens`. The rumble spike (ADR 0025) is an example program now, `cargo run -p flint-input-capture --example spike_rumble`; `flint spike-rumble` still runs it for one release. The calibration notice says `flint music calibrate`.
**What to do.** Change scripts, CI jobs and docs that call the old names to the new ones before the next release removes them.

### Frames are named for RenderDoc and PIX: `FLINT_GPU_LABELS`, `?gpu_labels=` (#1083)
**What changed.** A captured frame now reads in RenderDoc, PIX or Nsight as nested debug groups. They use the names the F2 overlay times (`Shadows`, `Scene`, each effect and style, `Composite`, `UI`). Inside them are a pass per shadow map (`Shadow cascade 0`, `Spot shadow`) and a group per layer of the draws (`Opaque static`, `Particles`, ...). Natively, groups are always on. Under a capture tool, or with `FLINT_GPU_LABELS=1`, each draw is also marked with its entity's name and kind (`barrel · Static`). `groups` and `0` give less. The web player adds none unless the page has `?gpu_labels=1`. No pixel changes. A host that times its own pass can open it with `flint_render::gpu_labels::begin` / `end` for a group of the same name.
**What to do.** Nothing. To capture a frame, see the book's Profiling guide, "Capturing a frame in RenderDoc".

### `tune_living(knobs, seconds)` eases Living Props knobs instead of stepping them (#514)
**What changed.** `tune_living` takes an optional last argument of seconds, as `set_wooze` does. With it, the continuous knobs (`step`, `glide`, `amplitude`, `jitter`, `echoes.spread`, `echoes.alpha`, `variance.amount`, `variance.steady`, `softness`) glide from where they stand to the target over that time on the field's smoothstep, for the whole grove or one species (`species: name`). The discrete ones (`echoes.count`, `stagger`, `variance.keep_size`, the boil) switch at the start. The grove's clock knobs ease on the wooze field's own ramps; the rest on the living clock, which a freeze or a paused state stops. A ramp started on a knob that's already easing starts from where it stands, and asking every frame for the same target still arrives on time. A knob set at once (no seconds, the F3 panel, a preset, `clear_living`) takes over from its ramp. `tune_living(map)` with no seconds is unchanged. In flint-core, `field::Ramp` is public, and `ParamField::global_target` / `WoozeField::global_target` say where the global knobs are heading.
**What to do.** Nothing. A script that eases a knob itself, calling `tune_living` every frame, can pass seconds once instead.

### Whole-frame ASCII over a few styled props costs less (#507)
**What changed.** With `[post_process.styles.ascii]` covering the frame and some
entities carrying an `ascii_style`, the style reads the style mask only near
those entities' screen bounds, and its tone statistics and per-cell pre-pass
work only there. On an RTX 4070 at 1080p (`examples/ascii_entities`, edges
off) a frame whose styled props are all `enabled = false` went from 0.066 to
0.057 ms (the plain frame is 0.056), and the demo with its normalised barrel
from 0.112 to 0.088 ms. Every pixel is the same as before but one fix: with
more than 255 per-entity ASCII variants and `footprint` 2 or more, a variant
past the 255th sharing a grid with an earlier one drew some cells smooth; it
is drawn in glyphs now. A Rust style can
ask the same question: `StyleFrame::mask_rect(pad)` is where the mask can hold
any of the style's ids, opt-outs included, and `variant_rect(id, pad)` one
id's; both are `None` when that could be anywhere.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### `[living] pose = "noise"`: space-colonization and grammar trees can live (#506)
**What changed.** A `tree_v1` spec's `[living]` table takes `pose = "noise"` and a `[living.noise]` table (`turn` in degrees, default 12; `wavelength` in metres, default 3; `stretch`, default 0.08). Each pose keeps the tree's own branches and bends them on smooth noise instead of regrowing them, so `branch_method = "space_colonization"` and text grammars (`lsystem_axiom`/`lsystem_rules`, `lsystem_preset`) now live, where `check_living` used to refuse them. On a colonized tree, `depth` in the living skeleton is now its branch order, which keeps per-level stagger holding on the beat. A tree with more segments than bones now folds a step off every twig in turn, not a whole twig at once. Trees that fold only a few tips fold as before. `form_v1` refuses `pose = "noise"`. A spec without the key bakes and renders as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. To bring a pine or willow to life, add `pose = "noise"` under its `[living]` (`examples/specs/grove_pine.procgen.toml`).

### Fog reaches translucent draws at their own depth: echoes, glass and particles no longer seam at a horizon (#513)
**What changed.** Depth fog (`fog_enabled`, height fog, the aerial tint and ground fog) was drawn from the depth buffer, which blended meshes, living props' echoes and particles don't write, so each of their pixels was fogged by whatever lay behind it: a near echo in front of the far edge of a floor was fogged as the floor below the edge and not at all above it, with a hard line between. Now, while the fog is on and a translucent draw is in view, the renderer records what lies behind them and a "fog veil" of their depth and coverage, and fogs their own light at their own depth. A translucent draw near the camera in thick fog looks more like itself, and one far off is fogged as far. Frames with no fog, or no translucent draw in view, are drawn exactly as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. A scene that turned fog off to hide the seam (as chrischaps/Embodiment's spike did) can turn it back on.

### A Rust style's labels and help go on its params: `StyleHint` is gone (#510)
**What changed.** `StyleParam` and `StyleOption` take `.label(..)`, `.help(..)`, `.when(..)` and `.unless(..)` (and a param `.integer()`) themselves; `StyleDescriptor::hints` and `StyleHint` are removed. A colour's label and help go on its `_r` channel. Read them back with `StyleDescriptor::label_for`, `help_for`, `is_integer` or `ui(name)` (the old `hint(name)`). F4, `--list-styles` and Copy TOML show the same labels as before. In a `.shader.toml`, a `label` or `help` on a colour's `_r` param now names the colour's picker, where before it named the red channel alone.
**What to do.** A game with its own Rust style moves each `StyleHint::new(name, label, help)` onto the setting: `StyleParam::new("grain", 0.5, 0.0..=1.0).label("Paper grain").help("...")`, then drops `.hints(..)`. Scene files and `.shader.toml` files need nothing.

### A spot light casts a shadow with `cast_shadows = true` (#504)
**What changed.** `[entities.x.light]` takes `cast_shadows` (a bool, unset is false). On a spot light it draws a shadow map from the light, softened by its `source_radius` the way a sun's `angular_size` softens the cascades: sharp where a caster meets the floor, wider as they part. It works with or without a sun. One spot casts, the first by entity name that asks; another that asks lights without one and logs `shadow-cap`, as does a point light asking (no point shadows yet) and the strongest directional light saying `false` (it casts the cascades regardless). Meshes, skinned meshes and terrain take the shadow; grass and the ocean don't. The shadow map texture holds one more layer, 16 MB at the default 2048 texels. A scene that doesn't ask renders as it did. Example: `examples/spot_shadow.scene.toml`.
**What to do.** Nothing. To give a key light its contact shadow, add `cast_shadows = true` to its spot, and drop any directional light you placed along it only for its shadow.

### An entity's components are held in name order (#1115)
**What changed.** `DynamicComponents::data` in `flint-ecs` is a
`BTreeMap<String, toml::Value>` (it was a `HashMap`). Walking it gives the same
order in every process, by name. The scene viewer's inspector and
`flint entity show` read it directly, so they listed an entity's components in
a new order each run. Now they list them in name order every time. Scenes,
scripts and saved files are unchanged.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless your game binary's Rust code names the field's
type (a `&HashMap<String, toml::Value>` taken from `components.data`). Change
that to `BTreeMap`, or read through `get`, `component_names` and `data.iter()`,
which work with either.

### Scripts read the wooze field and its beat: `wooze_at`, `wooze_beat`, `module_enabled` (#505)
**What changed.** The `wooze` script module could only change the field; now it reads it too. `wooze_at(x, y, z)` returns `#{ amplitude, step, glide, jitter, zones }` at a place, zones and scripts' changes included. `wooze_beat(x, y, z)` returns the hold-and-glide clock there, the one shallow wooze keeps: `#{ time, delta, beat, index, weight, amplitude, step }`, where `time` and `delta` hold still on the beat and stand in for `total_time()` and `delta_time()`. Both return `#{}` in a scene with no `[wooze]` table. The new core function `module_enabled(name)` says whether an optional feature's module (`wooze`, `living`, `debug`, `conducted`) can be called in the scene. The showcase's `creature_idle.rhai` bobs on the beat where a scene has `[wooze]`, and as before elsewhere.
**What to do.** Nothing. To make scripted motion hold with living props, read `wooze_beat` behind `module_enabled("wooze")` (the book's Wooze API).

### `--hitch-log`, `--max-hitches` and `[perf] hitches`: a run leaves a record of its hitches, and can fail on them (#1079)
**What changed.** `flint-player` and `flint play` take `--hitch-log FILE`, a JSON line for every hitch as the run goes (its frame, game and wall time, ms, the median it stood out from, missed refreshes, `player` or `elsewhere`, the worst phase and script, the engine's frame notes, and the eight frames either side). `--max-hitches N` exits non-zero past N hitches after the first two seconds of game time; hitches in those two seconds are counted apart as startup and held only to `--max-hitches-startup N`; `--max-hitches-of player|elsewhere` narrows both. `--exit-after`'s report gains `[perf] hitches:` and `[perf] startup hitches:` lines, and `--perf`'s line and `?perf=1`'s copied line gain a `hitches N in 10s (M in run), worst ...` part, before `cpu`. Either flag records every frame and times every script callback, as `--perf` does.
**What to do.** Nothing. A tool that parses the perf line by position should match parts by their first word, as `tools/web-perf/perfline.mjs` does.

### F2 shows a frame-time graph with hitch marks and counters; `--stats=graph` (#1078)
**What changed.** The player's F2 overlay opens with a graph of the last four seconds of frames, each bar split into scripts, physics, other, render, hud/present and elsewhere, with the display's refresh period and twice it drawn across, and a vermillion mark over every hitch. Under it are the session's hitches, hitches a minute, the worst frame, the time since the last hitch and what it was (`last 100 ms: stall.rhai:on_update took 100`). F2 now steps through three states: the full overlay, the graph alone in a corner, then off. `flint-player --stats` still opens the full overlay; `--stats=graph` opens the graph alone (the value needs the `=`). While F2 shows anything, scripts are timed as under `--perf`. A game that embeds `PlayerApp` can set `app.capture.stats_view = flint_player::StatsView::Graph` beside `show_stats`. The widget is `flint_debug_ui::widgets::FrameGraph`.
**What to do.** Nothing. A test or capture script that pressed F2 twice expecting it closed now needs three presses.

### Each recorded frame keeps notes of what the engine did on it, and a hitch names them as its cause; `flint_core::frame_notes` (#1077)
**What changed.** Model and texture loads, pipeline and style builds, scene transitions, chunk loads, script loads and hot reloads, procgen, audio decodes, surface reconfigures, frames of several physics steps and clock stalls now leave a note (kind, detail, ms) on the frame record they happened in. A hitch keeps its frame's notes and the frame before's, and `likely_cause()` names them or says `no engine event`. Nothing shows them yet (#1078, #1079). New API: `flint_core::frame_notes` (`note`, `timed`, `begin`, `listen`, `take`), a per-thread channel the player drains once a frame. A note where no one listens costs a flag read. `pipeline_cache::timed` is crate-private and now takes the label.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### A wrong type in an animator, sprite, terrain, material or 2D UI component is now said once (#802)
**What changed.** The animation, renderer and script systems read `animator` (and each of its `layers`), `sprite_animator`, `ik_two_bone`, `sprite`, `material`, `lod`, `bounds`, `model`, `terrain` and its `grass.*`, `grass_ground`, `sky`, `ocean`, `water_body`, `spline`, `spline_mesh`, `screen_anchor`, `parallax`, `ui_fill`, `ui_text`, `script` and `interactable` through the typed view, as physics and audio already did. A value of the wrong type, or an unknown `sprite.mode`, `material.blend_mode`, `screen_anchor.anchor`, `ui_text.align`, `ui_fill.direction` or layer `mode`, still reads as the default it always did, and is now logged once per entity and field (`error[bad-enum-value] entity 'hero' animator.layers[1].mode: ...`). Nothing behaves differently. For Rust: `SkyParams`, `OceanVisuals`, `OceanTint`, `WaterBodyLook`, `GrassConfig` and the new `TerrainConfig::from_component` take a `&ComponentView` (`world.view(id, name)`), and `ComponentView::tables(field)` reads an array of tables.
**What to do.** Fix what the log names. Rust that called one of those `from_component`s with a `toml::Value` passes `&world.view(id, name)`.

### The player keeps its last 600 frames and judges each for a hitch; the `frame-history` feature, `FLINT_FRAME_HISTORY`, `FLINT_HITCH_FACTOR`, `FLINT_HITCH_FLOOR_MS` (#1076)
**What changed.** With flint-player's new `frame-history` feature (on by default), the player records every frame, `--perf` or not. A record holds the phases, the clock's raw and paced time, present-to-present, the physics steps and the GPU time. Each frame is judged a hitch when it is over both 2x the rolling median and the median + 8 ms, on the paced clock under vsync. It costs about 0.65 µs a frame. Nothing shows it yet (#1078, #1079), and `--perf`'s output is unchanged. Without the feature, frames are recorded only under `--perf`, as before. New API: `GameClock::paced_delta_time()` and `SceneRenderer::take_gpu_frame_ms()`.
**What to do.** Nothing, to keep it. A shipped game drops it by building flint-player with `default-features = false` and the features it uses. `FLINT_FRAME_HISTORY=0|1` overrides the build at run time. `FLINT_HITCH_FACTOR` (default 2) and `FLINT_HITCH_FLOOR_MS` (default 8) set the thresholds.

### `[post_process] dither_style`: dither that hides banding in the dark (#503)
**What changed.** The composite's dither was added in linear light, before the target's sRGB encode, so near black it came out about 13 times as many display levels as in the midtones: any `dither_intensity` small enough to vanish in the light laid an 8x8 crosshatch over a dark scene. A new key, `dither_style`, says where it is added: `0` (the default) is linear light, exactly as before; `1` is the same Bayer matrix in display levels; `2` is triangular noise in display levels, with no tile. In styles 1 and 2, `dither_intensity` counts display levels over 255 at every brightness, so `0.004` is one 8-bit step. Scenes that set no style draw byte for byte as they did.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a dark scene bands: set `dither_enabled = true`, `dither_style = 2`, `dither_intensity = 0.004`. `examples/dark_stage.scene.toml` shows it.

### A `[living]` or `[wooze]` mistake is said once, and an unsettled preset is named (#807)
**What changed.** `flint validate` and `flint check` used to report one misspelt or mistyped key in `[living]` or `[wooze]` twice: an `unknown-field` warning (or `bad-type` error) naming the field, and a `toml-parse` error naming only the table. Now the first is the error, hint ending "the scene does not load until the table fits", and the second is gone. A `[wooze] preset` that names neither a `wooze/<name>.wooze.toml` file nor a built-in is now named by `flint_procgen::settle_scene` (and `apply_preset_file`) and taken out. A host that loads a scene and never calls `settle_scene` now gets a warning from the wooze field: `preset 'x' was never filled in ... this host didn't call it`. Before, it lost the preset file's knobs without a word.
**What to do.** A game binary that loads scenes itself: call `flint_procgen::settle_scene(&mut scene_file, path)` after `flint_scene::load_scene`, or call `PlayerApp::apply_scene_settings`, which does it for you. Anything that matched the old `toml-parse` text for these tables should match the field's diagnostic instead.

### `--sweep-crop` sheets show where the crop sits, and take fractions (#501)
**What changed.** A `flint render --sweep ... --sweep-crop` sheet now carries a locator in its header: the first cell's whole frame, shrunk, with the kept region outlined and the rest dimmed, so a reader sees which part of the frame every cell comes from. The crop can also be given as fractions of the frame, `--sweep-crop 0.4,0.48,0.33,0.33`, so one crop works at any `--width`/`--height`; a value with a decimal point makes the whole crop fractional, and each value must then be between 0 and 1. Pixel crops (`700,380,480,270`) mean what they did. A crop sheet is taller by the locator where its header was shorter than it, so a golden of a crop sweep needs re-blessing.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a game keeps a golden of a `--sweep-crop` sheet: re-bless it.

### Despawning an entity takes its children; `get_components_mut` returns a guard (#691)
**What changed.** `despawn_entity(id)` in a script, and `FlintWorld::despawn` / `despawn_by_name` in Rust, now remove the entity's whole subtree. Before, its children stayed as roots and jumped, since a child's transform is in its parent's frame. `despawn` returns the ids it removed, the entity's first. `FlintWorld::get_components_mut` returns a `ComponentsMut` guard instead of `&mut DynamicComponents`. Its `set`, `set_field`, `merge_component`, `merge`, `remove` and `set_archetype` keep the component index, it reads through `Deref`, and `get_mut(name)` changes one component's value in place. `world.get_component_mut(id, name)` does the same in one call. `rebuild_component_index` is gone: nothing can put the index out of step now. flint-ecs no longer depends on hecs or bimap.
**What to do.** A script that despawns a parent but wants a child kept calls `set_parent` on the child first. In Rust, bind the guard `mut` (`let mut comps = world.get_components_mut(id)?`). Write `comps.set_archetype(Some(..))` where you wrote `comps.archetype = Some(..)`, and pass a function that took `&mut DynamicComponents` the guard instead. Drop any `rebuild_component_index()` call.

### `flint validate` checks effect and sequence names, the asset catalog and lone colliders; `color` takes arrays only (#804)
**What changed.** `particle_effect.effect` and `animator.sequence` are `asset` fields now: a name no visible `particles/*.particles.toml` or `animations/*.sequence.toml` states as its `name` (an effect stating none goes by its file's stem) is a `missing-asset` error listing the folders read. A model or texture registered only in the project's `assets/*.asset.toml`, with its content in `.flint/assets`, passes where it was `missing-asset`, and `flint check` lets an `asset_def` name pass as `validate` does, so the two agree. A `collider` on an entity with no `rigidbody` (not a terrain or spline mesh) is a `needs-component` warning at validate and load, not only once the game runs. A `color` field written as a `{ r, g, b }` table or a `0xRRGGBB` integer is `bad-type`: no reader ever took either, so the colour was never drawn.
**What to do.** Run `flint validate` or `flint check`. Rewrite an old colour as the array the error gives (`[1.0, 0.533, 0.0]`), fix a misspelt effect or sequence name, and give a lone collider a `rigidbody` (`body_type = "static"` for scenery) or remove it.

### `[project.diagnostics] allow` in flint.toml, and the viewer lists a scene's diagnostics (#795)
**What changed.** A project can accept a diagnostic by its code, everywhere or at one place: `allow = ["unknown-field:material.wetness", "unknown-table"]` under `[project.diagnostics]`. What it names is left out of `flint validate`, `flint check`, every load's summary and the viewer, and each says how many (`2 warnings (1 allowed by flint.toml)`; `"allowed"` in the JSON). An entry naming no code is refused when the manifest is read, as is any other key of `[project.diagnostics]`. `flint edit <scene>` now lists the scene's diagnostics in its status panel, refreshed on every hot reload; clicking a row selects its entity. `[project] strict` is still not applied by a load (now documented): it changes what `flint validate`, `flint check` and the viewer report, not what the player logs.
**What to do.** Nothing. To quiet a warning your game means, such as a field only scripts read, either declare the field with `extend = true` (#794) so it is checked, or allow the code here so it is not.

### Rhai `get_look()` and `list_looks()`: a script asks which look is on (#497)
**What changed.** `get_look()` returns the look the scene draws with now: the one its `[post_process] look` names, `--look`, or the last that a script's `set_look`, F4's Look picker or a transition chose. It returns `""` for none, and a fade's target from its first frame. `list_looks()` returns every look the scene can see, sorted by name: the project's `styles/*.look.toml` files and the built-ins, as F4 lists them. `set_look` is still a queued command, so a read in the same call after it returns the old look, and from the next read after the player applies it (that frame's `on_draw_ui`) it returns the new one. In a `flint render --scripts` capture, a `set_look` whose look can't be found is now refused at the call with the player's `set_look:` warning. It no longer replaces an earlier request from the same frame.
**What to do.** A menu or HUD that kept its own copy of the current look can call `get_look()` instead, and that copy no longer goes stale when F4 or a scene change picks one. Nothing else.

### A scene's entity ids, and every walk of the world, are the same every run (#688)
**What changed.** Ids came from one counter shared by the whole process, and scenes spawned in `HashMap` order, so an entity's id changed from run to run and `entities_with_component` walked entities in a different order each time (a pile of dynamic bodies settled differently each load). Now each `FlintWorld` counts its own ids from 1 and a scene spawns in name order, so a scene's ids are its names' ranks in every process. A scene transition and an editor reload start past the last world's ids (`FlintWorld::after`, `flint_scene::load_scene_after`), so an id a script kept never names a new entity. `entities_with_component` returns a `BTreeSet` in id order; `all_entities`, `entity_names` and `get_children` are ordered too; `SceneFile::entities`, `prefabs` and `EntityDef::components` are `BTreeMap`s, so a saved scene lists entities and components by name. `EntityId::new()`, its `Default` and `ensure_counter_above` are gone.
**What to do.** Nothing for scenes and scripts. Rust that minted an id with `EntityId::new()` uses `EntityId::from_raw(n)` or `world.spawn`; code that named `HashSet<EntityId>` for `entities_with_component`'s result names `BTreeSet`. A seed meant to survive edits to the scene still comes from the entity's name: adding an entity moves the ids after it.

### A `bounds` box glows with `material.emissive`; `transform.rotation_quat` is declared; Android reads `[project] schemas` (#797)
**What changed.** An entity drawn from its `bounds` box now gives off its `material.emissive` (`[r, g, b]`, linear, so above 1 it blooms), times `emissive_scale`. The key used to be an `unknown-field` warning that nothing read. A box without it draws exactly as before. On a model the key is still not read: recolour a GLB's glow with `material_overrides`. `transform.rotation_quat` (`[x, y, z, w]`), which physics, IK and `set_rotation_quat` write and `Transform` always read, is now in the schema, so a scene that writes it gets no warning. On Android the schemas come from your packed `flint.toml`'s `[project] schemas`, as on desktop, so a game can list `engine/schemas/examples`. With no list it still loads `engine/schemas`, then `schemas`.
**What to do.** Nothing. If a scene set `material.emissive` on a box and you don't want it to glow, delete the key. An Android game that uses `furniture`, `wall` or the other room-builder archetypes adds `"engine/schemas/examples"` to its `[project] schemas`.

### A game adds a field to an engine component with `extend = true` (#794)
**What changed.** A schema file's `[component.<name>]` (or `[table.<name>]`) may say `extend = true`: its fields are then added to the component an earlier schema directory declared, instead of replacing it whole. A field named again is redefined; the earlier layer's description and fields stay. An extension of a name nothing earlier declares fails the load, naming the file and the nearest name. Without `extend`, a later layer still replaces a component whole, so existing files mean what they did. `flint schema <name>` prints `Declared in:` and `Extended by:` and tags each field with its file's number; `--format json` adds `declared_in`, `extended_by` and each field's `file`. The unknown-field hint now names the key. For Rust: `SchemaRegistry::component_origin` / `table_origin` return a `SchemaOrigin`.
**What to do.** A game that copied a whole engine component file to add a field can cut it to `extend = true` and its own fields, and stop tracking the engine's copy.

### `find_entities_with`, `get_children`, `overlap_rect` and `persist_keys` return the same order every run (#689)
**What changed.** The three functions that return entities now return them sorted by entity name, and `persist_keys` returns its keys sorted. They used to come in hash-map order, which changed from one run to the next, so `find_entities_with("enemy")[0]`, or a loop that spawned, played a sound or drew for each result, differed between two runs of the same scene and broke the byte-identical promise of `flint render --scripts` and `--frame-step` captures. `FlintWorld::get_children` (Rust) is name-ordered too and is now a lookup in a children index rather than a scan of every entity; `FlintWorld::in_name_order(ids)` sorts any list the same way, and `get_name` is a lookup.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a script relied on a particular order it happened to get: name entities for the order you want (`wave_01`, `wave_02`), or sort the array in the script.

### Spot lights light what they point at; lights say what they drop; `inner_angle_deg` / `outer_angle_deg` (#682)
**What changed.** Spot lights on meshes were evaluated backwards, so one pointing down lit nothing below it: they now light the way their `direction` says, which brightens any scene with a spot light. A light with no `direction` shines down its entity's -Y and turns with it and its parents (an unturned directional light with no `direction` is now a sun overhead, not one below the ground). Past the shader's 16 point or 8 spot lights, the ones nearest the camera are drawn, not the first by name, and each light left out is named once (`light-cap`); an unknown or missing `type` is said by entity name too. Spot cones take degrees as `inner_angle_deg` / `outer_angle_deg`; the radian keys still read as before.
**What to do.** Look at scenes with spot lights: they are brighter now, so check their `intensity`. Write new cones in degrees.

### A bad inline `particle_emitter` is an error naming its key, never the default emitter (#690)
**What changed.** `flint validate` and `flint check` hold every inline `particle_emitter` to the emitter's own reader: a key it doesn't read (`emision_rate`) or a value it can't use (`color = "orange"`, a zero `lifetime`, `shape = "donut"`) is a new `bad-emitter` error naming entity and key, so the run exits 1 where it used to warn or pass. Keys the emitter reads but the schema doesn't list (`lifetime = [a, b]`, `size` and `color` curves, a `shape = { kind = .. }` table) are no longer flagged in those commands. While playing, such a table used to become the default emitter (white dots, 10 a second); now a bad value set at run time leaves the emitter as it was, a table bad at load makes no emitter until fixed, and the warning names entity and key. In Rust, `EmitterConfig::from_toml` returns `Result<_, EmitterProblem>`, and `EmitterDef::KNOWN_KEYS` is `EmitterDef::known_keys()`.
**What to do.** Run `flint check` on your project and fix any `bad-emitter` it lists. Rust callers of `from_toml` handle the `Result`; replace `KNOWN_KEYS` with `known_keys()`.

### `flint edit` saves what differs from the file, prefab entities included, and logs each field to `.flint/viewer-edits.jsonl` (#679)
**What changed.** The scene viewer's Ctrl+S writes every field that differs from the file, so an undo after a save marks the scene dirty and the next save writes the value it put back. An edit to an entity a prefab instance makes is no longer dropped: it goes into `[prefabs.<instance>.overrides.<entity>.<component>]`.
