`[living]`, `[wooze]`, shallow wooze, woozy grass and their script modules.
A build without one links none of its crates (`--no-default-features` links
neither flint-music nor flint-procgen), and a scene that uses it prints one
line at its entry saying what won't run. flint-debug-ui's Living Props panel
is its own `living` feature, and the workspace now takes that crate with its
default features off.
**What to do.** Nothing for a game binary that takes `flint-player`'s
defaults. One that sets `default-features = false` adds the features its
scenes use, for example `features = ["music", "living"]`.

### `blend_source = "tear"`: props turn to their style only where reality tears, and render mode 6, the tear alone (#397)
**What changed.** A style component (`ascii_style`, `pencil_style`, `watercolor_style`, a user style's `<name>_style`) or a `style_mask` takes `blend_source`: `"own"` (the default, as before) or `"tear"`, which multiplies the entity's `blend` by the reality tear's mask at each pixel, so the prop is styled only where render modes 1-4 tear. Render mode 6 (`set_render_mode(6, mix)`, `--render-mode 6`, F4's "Tear (props)") is a tear with no look of its own: only those props show it. With no tear on, a torn prop is at blend 0 and costs nothing. `set_style_param(id, "blend_source", "tear")` and `get_style_param` take it. `pencil_style` and `watercolor_style` now declare `blend` too, which the framework already read. The tear's noise moved to `crates/flint-render/src/tear_mask.wgsl`, shared by the composite and the style mask; renders are unchanged.
**What to do.** Nothing. To have props tear into text, give them `blend_source = "tear"` and drive a tear from a script (`examples/ascii_tear.scene.toml`).

### A reality tear with no `mode_params` tears in patches (#395)
**What changed.** Render modes 1-4 read `mode_params.x` as the bleed mask's scale, and the config defaults it to 0. At 0 the noise was one value over the whole screen, so a partial `mode_mix` tore all of the frame or none of it. At `--mode-mix 0.55` with no params, the frame came out untouched. The shader now reads a scale of 0 or less as the default 3, the same way Matrix already reads a rate of 0 as 6. So `set_render_mode(1, 0.55)` with no `set_render_mode_params`, the F4 menu's starting values and `flint render --render-mode 1 --mode-mix 0.55` all draw drifting patches. They match `3, 0, 6, 0` byte for byte. Any positive scale draws exactly as before, and mode 5 (underwater, where `x` is eye depth) is untouched.
**What to do.** Nothing. A game that wants the whole frame to turn at once should drive `mode_mix` to 1, or use the radial iris (`y = 1`).

### `flint bundle <scene>` packs one scene with what it reaches; `flint check` reads glTF models' outside files (#382)
**What changed.** `flint bundle levels/x.scene.toml` (a scene file, not a folder) writes `levels/dist/x.flintpack` holding that scene and only the models, textures, sounds, scripts, prefabs and scenes it reaches, followed through scripts and prefabs to the end of the chain. It also holds the small kinds its search sees (animations, styles, looks, palettes, glyph sets, particle effects, specs, `scripts/lib/`), the project's `flint.toml` and its schemas. Paths are the project's, so `?bundle=x.flintpack` plays the scene as it plays from the disk. `--name` and `--out-dir` work as for a game; `--scene` is refused. Separately, `flint check`, `flint bundle` and `flint export web` now read each `.glb`/`.gltf` and fail on a texture or buffer it names beside itself that isn't there (the player used to skip the whole model silently), and read a music `*.suite.toml` for the stems it names.
**What to do.** Nothing. If `flint bundle` now fails with `the model names <file>`, pack that file (or embed it in the `.glb`).

### `tune_living`, `set_wooze`, `compare_living` and `conducted_*` are modules a feature owns: a call where it isn't running is an error (#698)
**What changed.** The `living`, `wooze`, `debug` (`commit_living` and the A/B) and `conducted` script modules are declared by the player's living props and music, and enabled in a scene where that feature runs: `living`, `wooze` and `debug` with living props, a `[living]` or `[wooze]` table, a `shallow_wooze` entity or woozy grass; `conducted` when the scene's `music_session` has a chart. There every call works as before, from `on_init` on (the player now builds a scene's subsystems before its scripts load, as `flint render --scripts` did). Anywhere else a call is a script error, `tune_living: the living module is not enabled here; it is enabled when ...`, which stops that callback, where before it logged once and went on. `flint script api` names each module's condition. In Rust, `ScriptSystem::set_conducted`, `flint_script::ConductedSnapshot` and the living `ScriptCommand` variants are gone: a module command is `ScriptCommand::Module`, and a script system with the player's whole API is `flint_player::script_system()`.
**What to do.** Nothing for a scene that has the feature. A script shared with scenes that don't should call these only where it does.

### The web player takes `?begin=auto`, and `tools/web-perf` measures a page with it (#381)
**What changed.** A web page opened with `?begin=auto` lifts its Begin gate as soon as the scene is ready, with no click: the game runs and draws as after Begin, but with no gesture behind it the browser refuses the pointer lock, starts sound only where autoplay is allowed, and the full-screen switch stays off. It is for drivers that measure a page, since a click sent over CDP never reliably reached the button. `tools/web-perf/run.mjs` is that driver, checked in: it serves a `flint export web` folder (or takes a URL), plays each scene of a bundle in headed Chrome at a fixed device-pixel canvas and each DPR asked for, and writes the `?perf=1` readout of every page as JSON, a screenshot each and a Markdown table of the GPU passes, bind groups and passes a frame.
**What to do.** Nothing. To measure your game in the browser: `flint export web <game> --out site`, then `node engine/tools/web-perf/run.mjs site games/<id>/<id>.flintpack <scene,...> --out perf` (`tools/web-perf/README.md`).

### `music_session` plays a suite with no chart; `music_beat()`, `music_bar()`, `music_section()`; the `rhythm` feature (#745)
**What changed.** A `music_session` needs only `manifest` now: its stems play on the suite's buses and scripts read the beat with `music_beat()` (beats from the first, negative in the count-in), `music_bar()` (from 0) and `music_section()` (`-1.0`, `-1`, `""` with no music). A new `events` field schedules an `*.events.toml`'s bus changes. Paths are from the project root. In play the beat follows the audio clock; under `--frame-step`, in `flint render --scripts` and with no device, the game clock, so captures are exact. The component has an engine schema (`flint schema music_session`). With a `chart`, the session is judged as before, now behind flint-player's `rhythm` Cargo feature (on by default): without it the player links neither flint-rhythm nor flint-input-capture and plays the suite unjudged.
**What to do.** Nothing for a scene. A game binary built with `default-features = false` adds `"rhythm"` to keep its charts judged. A game's own `music_session` schema can go: the engine's describes the same fields.

### `--perf` and `?perf=1` say when GPU times were taken under vsync (#380)
**What changed.** The `flint-player --perf` line, the web page's `?perf=1` panel and `--exit-after`'s `[perf] avg GPU` line now name the swapchain's present mode (`present fifo`, `fifo-relaxed`, `mailbox` or `immediate`) and say `under vsync` or `no vsync` beside the GPU time. Under vsync, when the GPU was busy for less than three quarters of the wall clock, they add `at idle clocks`: a GPU that waits for the display clocks down, and its pass times read high (1.4x on an RTX 4070 laptop, about 5x on DX12 in #270). `RenderContext::present_mode_in_use()` and `flint_render::resolve_present_mode` give the mode a host's surface runs in, with `Auto` modes resolved as wgpu does. A tool that parses the line finds a new `present …` part, and the GPU part is now `gpu <ms> ms <note>: <passes>`.
**What to do.** Nothing. To compare passes, time them with `FLINT_VSYNC=0` or `flint render --gpu-time`.

### `--style <name>:off` turns a style off everywhere, per-entity props included (#376)
**What changed.** `--style ascii:off` (and `ascii:enabled=false`) used to switch off only the scene's `[post_process.styles.ascii]` table: every entity with its own `ascii_style` (or a `style_mask` naming the style) stayed in glyphs, so the flag drew the same picture as no flag. It now turns the style off for the whole frame, its entities included, with bloom, SSAO, vignette and the rest of `[post_process]` as authored, and those entities' pixels belong to no style, so another whole-frame style draws over them as plain scene. This holds for every per-entity style (`pencil_style`, `watercolor_style`, a user style's `<name>_style`) on `flint render`, `flint play`, `flint edit`, `--sweep` columns and the web's `?style=`. A later `--style <name>` switches it back on. A scene table's `enabled = false` is unchanged: it is still the table's alone.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless a capture relied on `:off` leaving the props styled; use `--style ascii:scope=entities` for that.

### A suite names its own buses; the rhythm layer is `flint-rhythm` (#745)
**What changed.** A `*.suite.toml` may declare any buses, as many as it has, named as it likes, and they mix and list in the order it declares them. `[reintegration]` and each section's `pulse_window_ms` are optional: `flint validate-suite` and `flint render-suite` take a suite with neither. The rhythm contract (the six buses, `[reintegration]`, a window per section) is now `flint_rhythm::validate_manifest`; `validate-suite` applies it when a suite has `[reintegration]`, is given `--chart`, or with the new `--rhythm`. Charts, judgment, coherence, the ladder, reintegration, replay and haptics moved from `flint-music` to the new `flint-rhythm` crate; the latency files are `flint_music::latency`. `SuiteManifest::reintegration` is an `Option`, `Section::pulse_window_ms` an `Option<f64>`.
**What to do.** A game's Rust that names `flint_music::{chart_session, ladder, judgment, ...}` imports them from `flint_rhythm` (add `flint-rhythm` beside `flint-music`), and builds a `Reintegrator` with `Reintegrator::for_manifest(&manifest)?`. Rhythm fixtures call `flint_rhythm::validate_manifest`. Suite files need no change.

### `flint render --scripts` runs the player's own frame, so style reads keep up (#725)
**What changed.** A clocked `flint render` (`--time`, `--scripts`, living props, a `[wooze]` table) now steps the scene through the player's game core, the same code and frame order as `flint-player`, instead of a copy of it. Before, a script's `get_style_param` and `get_entity_style` read the frame's styles as they were at the last capture, so a whole-frame style a script changed mid-span read back stale until the next picture. Now they read what the player reads, the step after a change. On_update's commands are also done before `on_draw_ui` runs, and a game state that pauses `living` (#727) holds a capture's grove, as in the player; before, a capture's living props ignored it. Every example scene renders byte-identical to before; a scene that reads back styles it changes can differ, and now matches `flint-player --frame-step` at that time. `flint_player::HeadlessScripts`, `LivingRun` and `WoozeClocks` are gone: a tool that stepped a scene itself uses `flint_player::HeadlessGame`.
**What to do.** Nothing for scenes. A Rust tool built on those types moves to `HeadlessGame` (`new`, `start_living`, `start_scripts`, `step`).

### `flint render --script-time T` captures what a game's scripts make of a scene (#371)
**What changed.** `--script-time T` runs the scene's scripts to game time `T` and captures there: it is `--scripts --time T` in one flag, beside `--particle-time` and `--anim-time`, and takes what `--time` takes (`0..8 -o x.gif`, a list `2,5`) with `--frame-step` and `--fps`. Giving it with `--time` is an error. A render that doesn't run a scene's scripts now says so on stderr, naming the entities that carry them: a plain still used to say nothing. A render whose scripts ran no longer also warns "no living props, so only its other clocks move". The wording of the scripts warning in `--report json`'s `warnings` changed (it now names the flag).
**What to do.** Nothing. A check that greps a render's stderr for silence may now see the scripts warning on a scene with scripts; add `--script-time` to capture what they do, or expect the line.

### A game binary reaches the world, clock and scripts through `app.core` (#725)
**What changed.** `PlayerApp`'s game state moved into one windowless `GameCore`, held as `app.core` (ADR 0117 decision 4): `app.world`, `app.scene_path`, `app.clock`, `app.physics`, `app.animation`, `app.particles` and `app.script` are now `app.core.world`, `app.core.scene_path`, `app.core.clock` and so on. `app.input`, `app.audio`, `app.launch`, `app.scene` and `app.capture` stay where they were. A frame runs the same stages in the same order, so scenes, scripts and captures are unchanged. `flint_player::GameCore` is exported.
**What to do.** In a game binary that embeds `PlayerApp`, put `core.` in front of those seven fields, for example `app.core.script.seed_random(seed)` and `app.core.clock.frame_step = Some(step)`; to list your extension's script functions, `app.core.script.api_listing()`. Extensions are unchanged: `ExtensionCtx` still hands `pre_update` the world, clock and input.

### A debug key with nothing to act on says so on screen (#367)
**What changed.** Pressing F3 in a scene with no debug panels, F4 with no renderer, `` ` ``, `\` or F9 with no music session, or F8 with no living prop at the view centre used to log at `info`, which the player's log filter never prints, so the key seemed to do nothing. Now a short note shows at the top centre of the window for about two seconds (the key as a keycap, then what it found), fading out, and the console gets the same line as `[debug] F3: no debug panels in this scene`. F9's old `[music] no music session running — nothing to fail` line is now `[debug] F9: ...`. A capture under `--frame-step` shows the note on the same frames every run, since it runs on egui's clock. Builds without the `debug-hud` feature are unchanged.
**What to do.** Nothing. A test or tool that read F9's `[music]` line reads the `[debug] F9:` one instead.

### `register_state` refuses what nothing runs; `living` has its own key (#727)
**What changed.** A state names systems (`physics`, `scripts`, `animation`,
`particles`, `audio`, `living`), each `"run"` or `"pause"`. Any other key, any
other policy, and `rendering`, `transparent` and `"hidden"` (documented, never
implemented: the frame always draws) are now a script error at the call, and
the state isn't registered. Living props pause on `living`, not `animation`;
`paused` and `loading` pause everything, the grove included. A script's
`fire_event` now survives a scene transition. In Rust: `StateConfig` is a map
(`runs("physics")`, `with(..)`), `register_state` returns a `Result`,
`RuntimeSystem` is `initialize` and `update` only, and
`PhysicsSystem::fixed_update` takes the caller's `&mut EventBus`
(`push_event`/`drain_events` are gone).
**What to do.** Delete `rendering:` and `transparent:` from `register_state`
maps. Add `living: "pause"` to a state that should still the grove.

## 2026-11-11

### `PlayerApp`'s launch and scene fields are grouped: `app.launch.*`, `app.scene.*` (#724)
**What changed.** A game binary that builds a `PlayerApp` itself sets its
options through two groups. What `flint-player`'s flags set is
`app.launch` (`LaunchOptions`): `msaa_sample_count`, `window_size`,
`window_options`, `render_scale`, `quality_overrides`, `style_specs`,
`style_time`, `look`, `hold_at`, `save_dir`, `expression_feed`, `fullscreen`
and the rest. What the scene's file says is `app.scene` (`SceneSettings`),
with the `scene_` prefix dropped: `skybox_path`, `ambient`, `camera`,
`post_process`, `tables`, `preload_audio`, `input_config`, ... The `[living]`
and `[wooze]` tables are no longer public fields; read them with
`ExtensionCtx::scene_table`. Nothing a scene or script does changes.
**What to do.** In a game's `main.rs`, `app.msaa_sample_count = 4` becomes
`app.launch.msaa_sample_count = 4`, and `app.scene_camera = ...` becomes
`app.scene.camera = ...`; better, call `app.apply_scene_settings(&scene_file)`,
which sets every `app.scene` field from the file.

### `rumble(pad, strength, seconds)` buzzes a gamepad from any script; pads come from `flint_runtime::pad` (#748)
**What changed.** Scripts have a general `rumble(pad, strength, seconds)`: both motors of a pad (`-1` for every pad, else the index an input config's `gamepad = n` names) at 0..1 for up to 10 seconds, in gilrs's 50 ms ticks. A new call on a pad replaces what it was playing, and 0 strength or length stops it. It needs no music session (while one runs, the session's haptics own the motors and `rumble()` does nothing), and with no pad, a build without the `gamepad` feature or the browser it does nothing. The gilrs wrapper moved from `flint_input_capture::pad` to `flint_runtime::pad`, behind `flint-runtime`'s new `gamepad` feature (off by default; flint-player's `gamepad` turns it on). `flint_input_capture::pad` still re-exports it.
**What to do.** A game binary that names `flint_input_capture::pad` should name `flint_runtime::pad`; one that depends on `flint-runtime` and reads pads itself adds `features = ["gamepad"]`. Scripts: nothing.

### Script functions renamed to one convention, and every draw call takes an opts map (#702)
**What changed.** Names put the verb first and read a settable value with `get_`; aliases and `_ex` variants are gone. Every draw primitive has an opts form under its own name, `draw_rect(x, y, w, h, #{color, rounding, layer})`, a colour being `[r, g, b(, a)]` or `"#rrggbb"`; the positional colour forms stay. New: `touch_id(index)`, since `touch_x`/`touch_y` take a touch's id, not its index. The old names keep working for one release: `flint script api` lists them as deprecated and `flint script check` warns at each call.

| Old | New |
|---|---|
| `this_entity`, `log_info` | `self_entity`, `log` |
| `lod_scale`, `draw_distance_scale` | `get_lod_scale`, `get_draw_distance_scale` |
| `current_scene`, `current_state`, `state_stack` | `get_current_scene`, `get_current_state`, `get_state_stack` |
| `sprite_play`, `sprite_stop`, `sprite_set_speed`, `sprite_is_playing` | `play_sprite`, `stop_sprite`, `set_sprite_speed`, `is_sprite_playing` |
| `camera_follow`, `camera_follow_position`, `camera_follow_set` | `follow_with_camera`, `get_camera_follow_position`, `set_camera_follow_position` |
| `camera_shake`, `camera_shake_stop`, `camera_apply_shake` | `shake_camera`, `stop_camera_shake`, `apply_camera_shake` |
| `draw_text_ex`, `draw_text_stroked(_ex)`, `draw_text_opts` | `draw_text(x, y, text, size, #{color, stroke, layer, ..})` |
| `draw_rect_ex`, `draw_rect_ex4` | `draw_rect(x, y, w, h, #{color, rounding, layer})` (`rounding: [tl, tr, br, bl]`) |
| `draw_rect_gradient` (14 arguments) | `draw_rect_gradient(x, y, w, h, #{from, to, vertical, layer})` |
| `draw_rect_outline_ex`, `draw_circle_ex`, `draw_circle_outline_ex`, `draw_line_ex` | the base name with `#{color, thickness, rounding, layer}` |
| `draw_sprite_ex` | `draw_sprite(x, y, w, h, name, #{uv, color, layer})` |
| `draw_line_3d_ex`, `measure_text_ex`, `set_anim_layer_ex` | `draw_line_3d(.., #{..})`, `measure_text(text, size, #{font, spacing})`, `set_anim_layer(.., #{mode, mask})` |

**What to do.** Run `flint script check` on your scripts and move each warned call to its new name. A script looping over `touch_x(i)` for `i` in `0..touch_count()` should pass `touch_id(i)`.

### `load_scene()` reaches its scene when no script draws a fade (#362)
**What changed.** A scene transition used to wait in its Exiting phase until a script called `complete_transition()`, so a game with no HUD script drawing a fade froze on the old scene after `load_scene()` or `reload_scene()`, and every later `load_scene()` was ignored. Now, when no loaded script can call `complete_transition()` (none names it and none `import`s a module), the player loads the scene on the next frame and goes straight back to idle. Scripts that draw fades (`transition_phase()` plus `complete_transition()`, as `showcase_hud.rhai` does) behave as before. A phase that a script could end but never does is ended after 10 seconds of game time, with a warning.
**What to do.** Nothing. If your game worked around the freeze by calling `complete_transition()` every frame after `load_scene()`, you can delete that call.

### `flint script check`, and `flint check` / `flint validate` now fail on a script error (#694)
**What changed.** `flint script check [dir | scene | file.rhai] [--json]` compiles every script and the modules it imports, and reports at `file:line:col` a syntax error, a call of a function that isn't there (with the nearest name), a call with an argument count it doesn't take, a literal no declaration accepts, an `fn on_colision` that is no callback, a callback with the wrong parameters and an import that finds nothing. `flint check` and `flint validate` (and so `flint examples --check`) run the same pass, so a script error now fails them. Diagnostics' JSON gains `line` and `column`. The showcase pause menu's `draw_line` calls passed 8 arguments of 9 and never drew; fixed.
**What to do.** Run `flint script check` and fix what it finds. A function your game binary's extension registers isn't in the engine's API: pass `--allow <name>` for each, or `--api <file>` with your binary's own listing (`app.script.api_listing()` written in `flint script api --json`'s shape), to all three commands.

### `flint edit` draws a scene's `grass_ground`; its loader is `flint_render::load_scene_grass_ground` (#332)
**What changed.** The scene viewer now grows a `grass_ground` field on open and again on each hot reload (`--watch`), as `flint play` and `flint render` do, so a meadow is no longer bare in the editor. Editing the component's `grass.*` keys or switching it off with `"grass.enabled" = false` shows on the next reload. The loader moved from flint-player into flint-render, so the viewer can share it: `flint_render::load_scene_grass_ground(world, scene_path, ..)` and `load_grass_ground_in(world, project, scene_dir, ..)` return the `GrassConfig` and entity name they set (`LoadedGrassGround`). `flint_player::load_scene_grass_ground` keeps its signature and its `bool` and calls the new one.
**What to do.** Nothing. A game binary that loads a `grass_ground` itself can call the flint-render function instead and drop its flint-player dependency there.

### `flint render --scripts` does what the player does with styles, looks and the camera; `ScriptSystem`'s `take_*` methods are gone (#697)
**What changed.** Script commands go through one `ScriptHost` interface that
the player and `flint render --scripts` both implement. A capture now applies
`set_entity_style`, `set_style_param`, `tween_style_param`,
`clear_entity_style`, the whole-frame styles, `set_look`, `set_camera_roll`,
`set_render_mode`, `set_lod_scale`, `set_quality`, `reset_style_history`,
`clear_living` and the state functions, and `get_entity_style` reads there;
what it can't do (sound, the cursor, scene changes) is
`flint_player::PRESENTATION_ONLY`. The camera, post-processing, time scale,
low-pass, touch, LOD, cursor and quality requests arrive from
`ScriptSystem::drain_commands` as `ScriptCommand`s (`SetCamera`, ...);
`take_camera_overrides` and the nine other `take_*` methods are removed, and
`flint_player`'s `apply_style_command` / `apply_post_style_command` became
methods per command.
**What to do.** Nothing in a script. A game binary that called a `take_*`
method or matched `ScriptCommand` itself implements `flint_script::ScriptHost`
and calls `flint_script::host::apply_all`. Captures of scenes using these
functions change to show them.

### A schema file that doesn't parse names itself (#333)
**What changed.** A `components/*.toml` or `archetypes/*.toml` file of any schema directory that isn't valid TOML used to stop every command (`flint render`, `play`, `validate`, the player) with the parser's message alone: `TOML parse error: TOML parse error at line 2, column 21 ... expected newline, #`, and no file. The error now starts with the file's path, as a `scene/*.toml` table file's already did: `TOML parse error: schemas/components/style_mask.toml: TOML parse error at line 2, column 21 ...`. Files that load are unaffected. The engine's own schema files are now held by a test that loads each one (`crates/flint-constraint/tests/schema_files.rs`).
**What to do.** Nothing. A script that matched the old message text should look for `TOML parse error` and the file's path instead.

### The book's script API reference is generated, and `flint script api --check-book` keeps a book honest (#695)
**What changed.** The book has a Script API Reference page (Reference,
`docs/book/src/reference/script-api.md`): every function, its parameters'
kinds, what it returns, the conventions and the callbacks, written from the
engine's declarations by `flint script api --book`. The Scripting chapter's
function tables are generated the same way, between `script-api:begin` and
`script-api:end` comments. `flint script api --check-book [DIR]` exits 1 when
that text is stale or when a page calls a function the engine doesn't have,
or at an arity it isn't declared at (CI runs it on the engine's book). Fixed
calls the book made to functions that never existed: `action_held` (use
`is_action_pressed`), `entity()` (use `self_entity()`), and an undefined
`turn_view` and `current_mix` in two examples.
**What to do.** Nothing. A game with its own mdBook can check its pages
against the engine's API with `flint script api --check-book <book dir>`;
names there that aren't script calls go in `<book dir>/script-api-allow.txt`.

### `flint goldens`: golden image suites have their own command (#327)
**What changed.** A `*.goldens.toml` runs with `flint goldens <manifest>` and its flags (`--bless`, `--bless-variant`, `--bless-drifted`, `--case`, `--out-dir`, `--require-gpu`, `--isolate`); `flint goldens --help` lists only those. `flint render-suite` is the music suite's alone again: its help lists only the music flags, and `--output` is required by the parser (a missing one exits 2 with clap's usage line instead of a runtime error). The old spelling, `flint render-suite <x>.goldens.toml` with any golden flags, still works and runs the same command, so scripts and CI jobs that use it need no change. The suite's first line now reads `goldens '<name>': ...` and the contact sheet's heading `flint goldens`; `report.json` is unchanged.
**What to do.** Nothing. Prefer `flint goldens` in new scripts and docs.

### Debug builds load big models about 7x faster: copy the `[profile.dev.package.*]` overrides if your game has its own workspace (#326)
**What changed.** A debug build now optimizes what loads a texture: the PNG/JPEG decoders, glTF's JSON, SHA-256, and a new leaf crate, `flint-pixels`, that now holds the RGBA widening and the mip-chain box filter (`flint-render` calls it, and `flint_render::memoize_mip_chains` is unchanged). A debug `flint render` of `examples/style_mask.scene.toml` went from 19.3 s to 2.6 s (release: 2.1 s), with byte-identical pixels. Workspace crates stay unoptimized, so incremental rebuilds are as before.
**What to do.** If you build Flint through the engine's own workspace (`cargo run` in `engine/`, or a game crate that is a member of it), nothing. Cargo reads `[profile]` only from the root workspace, so a game whose own `Cargo.toml` is the root should copy the block under "A debug build optimizes what loads a texture" from `engine/Cargo.toml` into it.

### A `ui_text` font follows a scene transition into another folder (#312)
**What changed.** `ui_text.font = "<name>"` is found by the project's search from the scene's folder, but the renderer kept the first font a name loaded, along with its uploaded atlas (`font:<name>`). A game with `levels/town/fonts/pixel.glyphs.toml` and `levels/dungeon/fonts/pixel.glyphs.toml` drew the town's `pixel` in the dungeon after a transition. `SceneRenderer::load_ui_font` now remembers the scope its fonts came from: a load from another scope forgets them and their textures, and the next scene reads its own. Within one scene, and across scenes in the same folder, nothing reloads. The styles' glyph sets and palettes (the ASCII style's `font`) already followed the scene, since the player hands the renderer each scene's `AssetScope`. `SceneRenderer::loaded_ui_font(name)` is new: the font a scene's `ui_text` draws with, if it loaded.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### Every script function is declared: styles, particles, splines, ocean, saves and the rest take integers and check their entity (#695)
**What changed.** The rest of the script API is declared like the Entity
API, and `flint script api` lists each function's module (`debug` holds the
Living Props A/B calls and `commit_living`). A number takes an int
(`play_effect("spark", 0, 1, 0)`, `ocean_height(x, 0)`) and an int a whole
float (`emit_burst(id, 3.0)`), where both were "Function not found". The
particle emitter calls, the Style API's entity functions and `spline_*`
given `-1` or a despawned id do nothing, queue nothing and warn once.
`tween_style_param` with non-number seconds is a script error. A variable passed first to a function that takes a string, map or
array (`get_entity(name)`, `log(msg)`, `set_quality(settings)`) is left as
it was: since the declared API began it came back `()`. For Rust: the
`EmitBurst`, `SetEntityStyle`, `SetStyleParam`, `TweenStyleParam` and
`ClearEntityStyle` commands carry `entity: EntityId` for `entity_id: i64`,
and `ScriptCallContext`'s `unsafe` `world_ref`/`world_mut`/`physics_ref` are
gone (`try_world`, `try_world_mut`, `try_physics`).
**What to do.** Nothing for a script that worked; `.to_float()` added only
for these functions can go. Check a maybe-missing entity with
`entity_exists(id)`. Rust that matches those commands renames `entity_id`
to `entity`.

### `flint render --anim-time <s>` poses a scene's animated characters as the player has them at that time (#307)
**What changed.** `flint render` used to draw every skinned entity in its bind pose, so a character on an idle or walk clip stood in a T in snapshots and goldens. With `--anim-time 1.5` it now loads the scene's animation as the player does (skeletal clips, blend layers, sequences, `.anim.toml` property tweens, node and sprite clips), steps it to 1.5 s in fixed 1/60 s steps (`--frame-step` with `--time`), and runs two-bone IK after each step. Two renders at the same value give the same bytes. With `--time` or `--frames` the animation runs on with the clock from there, so `--anim-time 0 --time 0..4 -o walk.gif` is a walk cycle. Without the flag nothing changes, and the `--time` warning about `animator` entities now names the flag. A skinned entity with an `animator` and no `skeleton` component doesn't animate in the player either, and `--anim-time` now says so.
**What to do.** Nothing. To check a posed character headless, add `--anim-time <s>` instead of capturing from `flint-player --screenshot-at`.

### `flint render --debug-mode wireframe-overlay` works (#308)
**What changed.** `--debug-mode` now accepts `wireframe-overlay`, edge lines over solid PBR shading, the same image `--wireframe-overlay` draws. The render code had a branch for that name, but the argument parser refused it ("unknown debug mode"), so the branch could never run. The parser and both render paths now read one name-to-mode table, so a name can no longer be accepted in one place and refused in the other. The error for an unknown mode lists every valid name. `--wireframe-overlay` still works as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. A script or golden that passes `--wireframe-overlay` can keep it, or use `--debug-mode wireframe-overlay` instead.

### Draw calls, UI, sprites, the 2D camera and touch take integers; `draw_rect_ex(.., 0.0)` works (#695)
**What changed.** The UI Draw API (`draw_text*`, `draw_rect*`, `draw_circle*`,
`draw_line*`, `draw_sprite*`, rings, arcs, polygons, clips, `measure_text*`,
`screen_width`/`height`), the world draws (`draw_line_3d*`, `draw_world_line`,
`draw_world_text`), the screen-to-world queries (`world_to_screen`,
`screen_to_ground`, `screen_to_mesh`, `screen_to_heightfield`,
`ground_height`, `raycast_meshes`), the Data-Driven UI functions, the
screen-UI setters (`set_text`, `set_text_color`, `ui_set_value`,
`set_anchor`, `set_anchor_offset`), the Sprite API and sprite animator,
`camera_follow` and the shake, the touch functions, `move_character`,
`get_collider_extents` and `find_nearest_interactable` are declared like the
Entity API. A number takes an int (`draw_rect(10, 10, 100, 50, 1, 1, 1, 1)`)
and a `layer` or other int takes a whole float (`draw_text_ex(.., 0.0)`),
where both used to be "Function not found". A draw call with all-int numbers
is as fast as one with floats; a call mixing the two works but is slower in a
tight loop (the book's scripting page, API Reference). The sprite, screen-UI,
`camera_follow`, `move_character` and `get_collider_extents` functions now
warn once for `-1` or a despawned id, where they did nothing silently.
`find_nearest_interactable()` from a script's top level is a script error
("there is no world here") instead of a panic. The book's `ui_get_rect` keys
are corrected to `#{x, y, w, h}`, which is what it always returned.
**What to do.** Nothing. `.to_float()` calls added only to satisfy a draw call
can go. A new warning names a call on a missing entity: check it with
`entity_exists(id)` first.

### An animated skinned character stands at its model's own size: drop `rotation = [90, ...]` and the ~0.01 scale (#294)
**What changed.** A skeleton's pose now includes the glTF nodes above its root joint, as the glTF spec says. A Mixamo export keeps its centimetre-to-metre scale and Z-up turn on an `Armature` node there, and while a clip played that node was skipped, so the character came out 100 times too large and lying down. Scenes made up for it with `rotation = [90, yaw, 0]` and `scale` about `0.01` (`YBotWithIdle` at `0.015`). With that transform a character is now about 2 cm tall and lies on its back in the player. An entity with no `animator`, and `flint render`'s rest pose, look the same as before. Joint positions a `bone_probe` reads are now in the model's metres.
**What to do.** On every animated skinned entity, set the rotation's first value from `90` to `0` and multiply its scale by 100: `[90, -90, 0]` / `0.015` becomes `[0, -90, 0]` / `1.5`. For a human at life size, use scale 1.

### `fxaa = true` draws FXAA however the config was set (#297)
**What changed.** The renderer builds FXAA's pipeline and intermediate texture on the first frame the config asks for it, as it already did for styles (#262). Before, only the F4 menu and a resize built them, so `flint edit` drew no FXAA after a hot reload that turned `fxaa` on (the frame stayed byte-identical to FXAA off), and a game that switched `fxaa_enabled` through `SceneRenderer::set_post_process_config` without calling `ensure_fxaa_resources` drew none until the window was resized. FXAA set at load looks the same as before.
**What to do.** Nothing. A game binary may drop its own `ensure_fxaa_resources` calls; keeping one after setting a config still moves the shader compile off the frame that first draws FXAA.

### `flint render --gpu-time` prints min/p50/p90/max per pass; `--gpu-time-json` (#292)
**What changed.** `--gpu-time N` used to print each pass's exponentially smoothed time, which hid how much the frames varied. It now keeps every frame's raw times, drops the first tenth (at most 30) as warm-up, and prints a table of `min p50 p90 max` per pass plus the frame total. Its first line names the adapter and backend (`on NVIDIA ... (Vulkan):`), so a `WGPU_BACKEND` run shows which one it measured. The new `--gpu-time-json <file>` writes the same with each pass's mean and raw `samples_ms`. In flint-render, `GpuTimer::record(on)`, `recorded()`, `flush(device)` and `gpu_timer::spread(frames, drop)` give a host the same numbers; `HeadlessContext` gained `adapter_info`. The player's smoothed overlay is unchanged.
**What to do.** A script that parsed the old one-number-per-pass lines should read `--gpu-time-json` instead, or take the `p50` column.

### Input, time, audio, animation, physics, camera, post-process, math, event and log functions take integers; `log(42)` logs (#695)
**What changed.** These groups are now declared like the Entity API: a number
parameter takes an int (`set_camera_fov(60)`, `sin(1)`, `play_sound("hit", 1)`),
an int parameter takes a whole float (`is_mouse_pressed(0.0)`), and
`play_clip`, the animation layers, `audio_position`, `set_velocity_2d` and
`get_velocity_2d` warn once for `-1` or a despawned id and then do nothing
(`set_velocity_2d` no longer queues one). `log`, `log_warn`, `log_error` and
`log_info` take any value. `log(x)` with one float used to be Rhai's base-10
logarithm, silently; it now logs the number. `flint script api` opens with
these rules (`conventions` in `--json`).
**What to do.** If a script used `log(x)` as a logarithm, write `log(x, 10.0)`.
A new warning names a call on a missing entity: check it with
`entity_exists(id)` first.

### A game's own procgen generators, through `PlayerExtension::register_generators` (#195)
**What changed.** A game binary can now give the player procgen generators the engine doesn't ship. `PlayerExtension` has a new hook, `register_generators(&mut flint_procgen::GeneratorRegistry)`, called once from `add_extension` with the registry the player resolves every scene's `*.procgen.toml` through; it already holds the built-ins. A spec whose `generator` names a type registered there resolves like a built-in's: generated at load or queued, cached in memory and on disk, ahead of a model file of that name. Registered under a built-in's name, it replaces the built-in. `ProcGenResolver` has `registry()`, `registry_mut()` and `with_registry(config, registry)`. Only the player sees a game's generators: `flint gen`, `flint edit`, `flint render` and the stock `flint-player` know the built-ins alone. ADR 0119; example `crates/flint-player/examples/cave_game/`.
**What to do.** Nothing, unless you want one: implement the hook in your extension and add the extension before the event loop starts.

### `flint script api` lists the script API; Entity functions take integers (#695)
**What changed.** `flint script api` prints every function a script can call
and the callbacks; `--json` is the same as data, `--filter draw` narrows it.
The Entity API (`set_position`, `get_component`, `set_material_override`, ...)
is declared through the engine's new API builder (ADR 0116): an integer works
wherever it wants a number (`set_position(id, 0, 1, 0)`), a wrong argument is
an error naming the function, the parameter and the line, and every Entity
function given `-1` or a despawned id warns once per script, getters included
(they still return their empty value). A game's `PlayerExtension` can declare
functions the same way with `declare_script_api`.
**What to do.** Nothing. A new warning names a getter that was quietly reading
a missing entity: check it with `entity_exists(id)` first.

### A scene reached by `load_scene()` starts as it would at startup (#723)
**What changed.** A transition now runs the same bring-up as the first scene, so the new scene's `[scene] input_config` is loaded (it was ignored), and a scene with no `[post_process]`, no `[environment] skybox`, no ambient or no `[camera]` gets the defaults instead of the last scene's bloom, fog, styles, script post overrides, skybox, lighting and camera. `on_init` at a transition can reach the persistent store and state machine. A target that can't be loaded no longer leaves an empty world: the current scene keeps playing, the error names the path it was read from, and the run exits non-zero. `flint play` now takes the scene's ambient, shadow and shading settings, as `flint-player` always did. `PlayerApp::scene_post_config()` returns a `PostProcessConfig`, not an `Option`.
**What to do.** A game that relied on a setting carrying over must author it in each scene that needs it. Drop `.unwrap_or_default()` after `scene_post_config()`.

### Living props' scene types moved from `flint_scene` to `flint_procgen` (#686)
**What changed.** For a game with its own Rust binary only; scenes and
scripts are untouched. `LivingDef`, `LivingSpeciesDef`, `LivingEchoesDef`,
`LivingVarianceDef`, `WoozeDef`, `WoozePreset`, `WoozeKnobs`, `BoilDef`,
`WoozeIssue`, the preset functions (`read_preset`, `write_preset`,
`list_presets`, `apply_preset_file`) and `commit_look` / `look_edits` are
`flint_procgen::` now. `SceneFile` has no `living` or `wooze` field: the two
tables are in `scene_file.tables`, read with
`flint_procgen::LivingTables::of(&scene_file)`. `flint_scene::load_scene` no
longer fills in the file a `[wooze] preset` names.
**What to do.** Change the paths. After `load_scene`, call
`flint_procgen::settle_scene(&mut scene_file, path)?`; a binary that calls
`PlayerApp::apply_scene_settings` gets the preset filled in without it. A
misspelled key in `[living]` or `[wooze]` still refuses the scene, now naming
the table instead of a line number.

### Scene-level tables have schemas, and a misspelled one warns (#686)
**What changed.** A scene's top-level tables (`[camera]`, `[environment]`,
`[post_process]`, `[living]`, `[wooze]`) are declared as `[table.<name>]` in
`schemas/scene/*.toml` and checked like components, by every scene load and
`flint validate`. A table no schema declares warns as `unknown-table`
(`[enviroment]`: did you mean `environment`?), a key a table doesn't declare
as `unknown-field`, and a bad value is an error: `[camera] projection =
"ortho"` used to draw in perspective without a word. `--strict` makes both
warnings errors. A game's own table (`[weather]`) is now kept by the loader:
declare it in `schemas/scene/weather.toml` and read it from a
`PlayerExtension` with `ctx.scene_table("weather")`. `wooze` and `wooze_zone`
are no longer components (`flint schema wooze` shows the table and its zones).
**What to do.** Run `flint validate` and read the new warnings; each names a
key that was doing nothing. Declare your own top-level tables. A game schema
that overrode `[component.wooze_zone]` becomes `[table.wooze]`'s `zone` field.
[Schemas](https://docs.chaps.dev/concepts/schemas.html#scene-level-tables) has the rest.

### `flint edit` saves a `.procgen.toml` without losing its comments (#194)
**What changed.** `Ctrl+S` and `Ctrl+Shift+S` in the procgen previewer, and `Ctrl+S` in the texture pipeline editor, used to rewrite the whole spec with `toml::to_string_pretty`. That dropped every comment and sorted the keys alphabetically. Now they patch the file you opened. A changed value is replaced on its own line and keeps its trailing comment. A key the editor drops moves the comment above it to the next key. A new key goes at the end of its table. A save with nothing changed leaves the file byte for byte, and CRLF files stay CRLF. The node editor no longer writes the defaults it fills in, or an `id` and `inputs` on every op, unless the file used them or the graph needs them. Specs an earlier save already flattened stay as they are.
**What to do.** Nothing.

### `flint check`: one pre-flight for a project; a constraint with a bad query now fails, and so do a few other things that passed in silence (#687)
**What changed.** New command: `flint check [dir|scene] [--json] [--strict]`
lists everything wrong with a project in one run (schemas, entity references,
every asset and file a scene names with the paths tried, styles, constraints)
and exits 1 on an error. `flint validate` and `flint bundle` run the same
checks, and `flint init` prints `flint check` as its first next step.

What now exits non-zero that did not:

| Case | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A constraint whose `query` (or `query_rule` `rule`) does not parse, or selects `components` | skipped: it passed every scene | an error naming the constraint, its file and the parse error, in `flint validate`, `flint check` and the viewer |
| A query with text after its one condition (`entities where a == 1 and b == 2`), in `flint query` too | read as `a == 1`, the rest dropped | a parse error: there is no `and`, `or` or `not` |
| A `joint.parent` naming no entity (the schema now types it `entity`, as #684 typed `ik_two_bone`'s); an `ik_two_bone.tip` that is not a child of its entity | exit 0 | `error[unknown-entity]` / `error[not-a-child]`, exit 1 |
| `flint validate --fix` with an error-level violation no fix covers; `--dry-run` on a scene with one | exit 0 | exit 1: the exit code says whether the file, as the run left it, has an error |
| `flint validate --format` other than `text` or `json` | printed text | refused (exit 2) |
| `flint bundle`: a model whose only source is a `<name>.procgen.toml` that is empty or does not parse | packed | `error[bad-spec]` or `error[missing-asset]` |

A scene with none of these passes `flint validate` as before. Also:
`--fix` and `--dry-run` honour `--format json` (a `fix` object in the same
document); each JSON diagnostic has a new `tried` array; `flint bundle`'s
refusal lists problems as `flint check` prints them.
**What to do.** Run `flint check`. Rewrite each `bad-constraint` query as one
condition (it was never being checked, so expect real violations once it
runs); fix or empty (`""`) each dangling `joint.parent` and IK field. A CI
step that reads the exit code of `flint validate --fix` or `--dry-run` now
gets "the scene still has an error". Use `flint check` as the gate before
playing or bundling.

### `collider`, `rigidbody` and nine other schemas load their fields; a schema file with keys outside `fields` fails; `audio_listener` is gone (#792)
**What changed.** Ten engine schemas (`collider`, `rigidbody`, `character_controller`, `audio_source`, `audio_trigger`, `joint`, `parallax`, `screen_anchor`, `ui_fill`, `ui_text`) declared their fields under `[component.x]` and loaded with none. They now load, so `flint validate` checks them: `collider.shpae` warns, `rigidbody.body_type = "dynamc"` is an error, and `audio_source.bus`, `screen_anchor.anchor`, `ui_fill.direction` and `ui_text.align` are enums. Their defaults are written in at load and each equals the reader's fallback, so nothing plays differently. A component schema file with any key under `[component.x]` besides `description` and `fields` now fails to load, naming the file and keys. `audio_listener` is deleted: nothing read it, since the listener is the camera, and the `player` archetype no longer lists it.
**What to do.** Run `flint validate` on your scenes. Delete any `[entities.x.audio_listener]` table, which now warns as an unknown component. If one of your own schema files fails to load, move its fields under `[component.<name>.fields]`.

### Schemas check references: `entity` and `asset` fields, exact-length vectors, `flint schema --list` and a JSON Schema (ADR 0115, #684)
**What changed.** `flint validate` (and the player's load summary) now
reports two new errors: `unknown-entity` for a field or `parent` that names
no entity (`ik_two_bone.target`, `spline_mesh.spline`), and `missing-asset`,
with every path tried, for `model.asset`, `script.source`, `material` and
`sprite` textures and the spline, terrain and grass files. `""` means "not
set". `material.blend_mode`, `sprite.mode` and `particle_emitter.blend_mode`,
`.shape`, `.sort` are enums. `vec2`/`vec4` are exactly 2 and 4 numbers; a
`color` is 3 or 4. Schema files gain `entity`, `asset` (with `kind`) and
`table` with `fields`; `float`, `boolean`, `integer` and `entity_ref` still
load, with an `old-type-name` warning for your own files. `flint schema`
gains `--list`, `--format json` and `--format json-schema --output <file>`
(editor completion), and exits 1 for an unknown name.
[Schemas](docs/book/src/concepts/schemas.md) has the type list and codes.

**What to do.** Run `flint validate`: a reference it flags was already
dangling, so fix the name, add the file, or set `""` when a script fills it.
A `vec2`/`vec4` field of yours holding another count becomes
`{ type = "array", element = "f32" }` or `color`. Respell old type names when
warned.

### Physics and audio say when a value is wrong, and an integer is a number (ADR 0115, #685)
**What changed.** Physics and audio now log a wrong component value once per
entity and field instead of taking the default in silence: an unknown
`rigidbody.body_type`, `collider.shape`, `joint.type` or `audio_source.bus`
(with the allowed values and the nearest one), a value of the wrong type, and
a `collider` on an entity with no `rigidbody`, which has never collided
(`needs-component`). What they fall back to is unchanged. Two behaviours do
change: `character_controller.move_speed`, `jump_force` and `height` written
as integers (`move_speed = 8`) used to read as the defaults (5.0, 6.0, eye
height 1.5) and are now read as written, and `material.base_color_r/g/b/a`
written as integers used to drop the tint and now apply it.

**What to do.** Run the game and read the log for `flint_ecs::view` lines;
each says what to write. If a character moves or jumps differently, or a
model gains a tint, the scene had an integer the engine ignored: write the
value you want. Rust in a game binary can read the same way with
`world.view(id, "collider").f32("friction", 0.5)`
([book](../docs/book/src/guides/player-extensions.md#reading-components)).

### The changelog is a page of months; `scripts/changelog.sh since <date>` prints what is new (#788)
**What changed.** `CHANGELOG.md` no longer holds the entries: it is a short
page that links one file a month, `changelog/YYYY-MM.md`, newest first. The
entries up to 2026-11-11 are in `changelog/2026-11.md`, unchanged. An entry
waiting to be assembled is a file in `changelog.d/`.
**What to do.** After an engine pull, run
`bash engine/scripts/changelog.sh since <the day of your last pull>` instead
of reading `engine/CHANGELOG.md` top down. It needs bash and git, as the
pull does. A game's agent guide written by an older `flint init` still says
to read `CHANGELOG.md`; that page now says where to look.

### Save data moves to the OS user directory for a game with an id; saves are atomic, a corrupt file is kept as `.bak`, and there are slots and a version (#726)
**What changed.** Where the `persist_*` store is saved, how it is written, and
what a script can ask of it.

**Where saves live.** Decided once at startup; no scene transition moves it.

| The game | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| `flint.toml` has `[project] id = "kart"` | `<project>/save/persist.toml` | `<config>/kart/saves/default.toml` |
| no manifest id, but the input config sets `game_id = "kart"` | `<project>/save/persist.toml` | `<config>/kart/saves/default.toml` |
| no id at all, or an id that can't be a folder name | `<project>/save/persist.toml` | the same file |
| the web | `localStorage`, `flint:/config/flint/save_<game_id>.toml` | the same entry, keyed on `[project] id` when the manifest has one, else on `game_id` as before |
| Android | the app's files directory, `files/save/persist.toml` | the same file |

`<config>` is `%APPDATA%` on Windows, `~/.config` on Linux and
`~/Library/Application Support` on macOS, where input rebinds already go.
Nothing is written into the game's folder any more, so a game installed
read-only can save.

**The old file is read once and left in place.** When a game with an id starts
and has no `default.toml` under the user directory, the player reads
`<project>/save/persist.toml` (then, for a scene two folders deep, the older
guessed place #706 already read), logs one warning saying where the save now
lives, and writes it there within a second. The file in the project is not
changed, moved or deleted, and is not read again once the new one exists. On
the web, when the manifest's `id` differs from the input config's `game_id`,
the entry keyed on `game_id` is read once the same way. These reads last one
release.

**Writes are atomic.** Every save is a temp file (`<file>.tmp`) flushed and
renamed over the save, so a crash or power cut mid-save leaves the old file
whole. Input rebinds are written the same way. A save now costs a disk flush
(about 4 ms measured, against 0.2 ms), at most once a second.

**A save file that doesn't parse is kept.** It used to be logged, ignored and
then overwritten by the next save. Now it is renamed `<file>.bak`
(`.bak.1`, `.bak.2`, ... if that name is taken), an error names both paths,
the game starts with an empty store, and `persist_corrupt()` returns
`#{ slot, file, backup, error }` so a menu can tell the player.

**New in the script API.**

| Function / callback | What it does |
|---|---|
| `persist_slot()` | the slot held, `"default"` at startup |
| `persist_slot(name)` -> `bool` | saves the slot held if it changed, then loads `name` (empty if new). Names: 1-32 of `a-z 0-9 _ -` |
| `persist_slots()` | the slots that have a save, in name order |
| `persist_version()` | the version of the save loaded; 0 for a file with no `[meta]` |
| `persist_set_version(n)` | stamp it by hand (a game with no manifest) |
| `persist_corrupt()` | `()` or the report above |
| `fn on_persist_migrate(from)` | called before `on_init` when the save loaded is older than `[project] save_version`; the store is stamped with the game's version afterwards |

**New manifest key**: `[project] save_version = <whole number>` (0 when left
out). **New flag**: `--save-dir <dir>` on `flint play` and `flint-player`
keeps a run's saves in `<dir>/<slot>.toml` and reads no other save.

**The file format** is unchanged for existing saves: a file with no `[meta]`
table is version 0 with every top-level key the game's, and a store at
version 0 is still written without one. The engine keeps the one top-level
name `meta` (`[meta] version = 2`). A game key called `meta` keeps working
(it is saved as `meta.key_meta`), with one exception: an old save whose
`meta` key was exactly a table holding only an integer `version` is read as
the engine's table.

**What to do.**
- A game with an `id`: nothing, the save follows on first launch. Tell
  players (and your own tooling) that saves are under `<config>/<id>/saves/`
  now; anything that read or deleted `save/persist.toml` in the project to
  reset progress should look there, or pass `--save-dir`. Once every player
  has launched the new build, the old `save/` folder can go.
- A game with no `id`: nothing changes. To move its saves out of the project,
  add `id = "..."` under `[project]` in `flint.toml`.
- A `log`/CI check that greps for `persist: failed to load`: the message is
  now an `error` that names the `.bak`.
- A script that stores a key named `meta` holding only `#{ version: <int> }`:
  rename the key before upgrading.

### Scenes say everything that is wrong with them, and `flint validate` fails on it (ADR 0115, #683)
**What changed.** A scene is checked against the schemas in one pass that
never stops at the first problem, and each problem names its entity,
component and field, with a suggestion:

```
scenes/hall.scene.toml: 4 errors, 1 warning

  error[unknown-archetype] entity 'hero' field 'archetype': no archetype named `playr`, so the entity gets no defaults
      hint: did you mean `player`?
  error[bad-type] entity 'hero' transform.scale: expected vec3, got string
  error[unknown-component] entity 'lever' component 'interactible': no component named `interactible`, so nothing checks it
      hint: did you mean `interactable`?
  error[bad-enum-value] entity 'pond' water_body.shape: "lak" is not a value of `shape`
      hint: did you mean `lake`? allowed values: lake, river
  warning[unknown-field] entity 'hero' transform.postion: `transform` has no field `postion`
      hint: did you mean `position`?
```

- **Errors:** a value of the wrong type (`bad-type`), a string that is none
  of an enum's values (`bad-enum-value`), a number outside `min`/`max`
  (`out-of-range`), a required field that is missing (`missing-field`), a
  component no loaded schema directory declares (`unknown-component`), an
  archetype none declares (`unknown-archetype`), a file that is not TOML
  (`toml-parse`, which now names the file, as the loader's own error does).
  Until now an unknown archetype or component was skipped in silence, and
  the rest stopped at the first problem per component without naming the
  field.
- **Warning:** a field its component's schema doesn't declare
  (`unknown-field`). It used to be allowed without a word.
- **`flint validate <scene>`** prints the whole list (text, or
  `--format json` with a `diagnostics` array beside `violations`) and
  **exits 1 on any error**. It now loads and checks the scene even when no
  schema directory has constraint files; it used to print "No constraints
  found" and exit 0 without reading the scene's entities. JSON's `valid`,
  `errors` and `warnings` count both lists.
- **`flint validate --strict`**, or `strict = true` under `[project]` in
  `flint.toml`, makes an unknown field an error.
- **The player and every command that loads a scene** (`flint play`,
  `flint-player`, `flint render`, the viewer) log one summary line and the
  first five problems at each load, transitions and chunks included, and
  carry on: nothing that loaded before stops loading. The per-component
  `[scene] entity '<name>' component '<comp>': <reason>` warnings are gone.
- `flint examples --check` fails an example on an error diagnostic. The
  engine's examples are clean of them; the components only their scripts
  read (`film`, `story`, `tour`, `orbit`, `hologram_style`) are open schemas
  in `schemas/examples/components/`.
- The engine's `material` schema now declares `color` and
  `base_color_r/g/b/a`, which the renderer always read.

**What to do.** Run `flint validate` on your scenes; expect it to fail where
it used to pass.

- A typo: fix it; the hint usually names what you meant.
- **A field of your own on an engine component** (read by your scripts):
  declare it in your schema layer by overriding the component file with the
  engine's fields plus yours (a later schema directory replaces a component
  of the same name whole), or keep it in a component of your own. Until then
  it is a warning and nothing fails, unless you turn `strict` on.
- **A component of your own** (in a scene, read only by scripts): declare it
  in your schema layer. One with fields is checked; one with a name and no
  fields is known and unchecked:

  ```toml
  # schemas/components/inventory.toml
  [component.inventory]
  description = "Read by scripts/inventory.rhai"
  ```

  A component a script attaches at run time and no scene file writes needs
  nothing.
- **An `archetype = "..."` used as a label** with no archetype file: add
  `schemas/archetypes/<name>.toml` (`[archetype.<name>]` and a `components`
  list), or drop the line.
- A game binary that embeds `PlayerApp`: `set_schema_paths` and
  `set_schema_dirs` work as before. `set_schemas(registry, dirs)` hands over
  the registry you loaded the first scene with, so the player never reads it
  again (see the next entry).
- Rust callers: `FlintError::InvalidFieldType` and `InvalidEnumValue` gained
  a `field`; `flint_schema::validate_component` returns every problem as
  `flint_core::Diagnostic`s, and `flint_scene::diagnose` does it for a file.

### A scene transition never loads with an empty schema registry (#683)
**What changed.** The player re-read every schema directory at each scene
transition and each `load_chunk`, and when that read failed (a schema file
that had stopped parsing, say) it loaded the scene with an empty registry
and logged one line: archetype defaults and schema defaults vanished for
that scene. The registry is now read once and kept. A chunk uses it as it
is; a transition reads the disk again only when the next scene's schema
directories differ (another project's `flint.toml`, or a `schemas/` beside
the new scene), and a read that fails logs an error and keeps the registry
already loaded. In the web player, the engine schemas compiled into the
player now survive a transition too.

**What to do.** Nothing. One thing to know: a schema file edited while the
game runs is no longer picked up by the next transition; restart the player.

### `flint entity create/delete` edit one table and leave the rest of the file alone (#678)
**What changed.** Both commands used to re-serialise the whole scene: a
string with a `:` in `--props` was rewritten (`"a:b.glb"` became
`"a=b.glb"`), every prefab instance was baked into plain entities and
`[prefabs]` emptied, every `[post_process]` default was written out (which
stopped a scene's `look` from applying), comments were dropped and entity
order changed on every run. Now `create` adds only `[entities.<name>]` after
the last entity, and `delete` removes only that entity's tables. `create`
writes the archetype, parent and props given, not the archetype's defaults
(they're filled in at load, as before). Deleting an entity with children is
an error unless you pass the new `--recursive`, which deletes the subtree.
Either command refuses, and leaves the file as it was, when the edited scene
would not load or a prop doesn't fit its component's schema; a `null` in
`--props` is now an error.
**What to do.** Nothing for scenes. A script that deleted a parent and
relied on the children staying (they then broke the next load) must delete
the children first or pass `--recursive`. A scene that a past `entity`
command baked or filled with defaults stays as it is; `git log -p` on it shows
what was lost.

### The room-builder archetypes left the engine's schema set; `model`, `light`, `spline` and `spline_mesh` have schemas (#681)
**What changed.** Three things, of which the first can stop a game from
bundling.

1. **`furniture`, `wall`, `floor`, `ceiling`, `pillar`, `room` and `door`
   (the archetypes), the `door` component and the three constraints about
   rooms and doors are no longer in `engine/schemas`.** They are the
   vocabulary the engine's example scenes are written in, and they moved to a
   layer of their own, `engine/schemas/examples`. In a game that lists only
   `engine/schemas`:
   - `flint bundle` and `flint export web` refuse a scene that names one:
     `archetype "furniture" (in no bundled schema)`;
   - `flint entity create --archetype room` fails with `Unknown archetype`,
     and `flint schema furniture` finds nothing;
   - at load, an entity that names one takes no defaults from it. One that
     writes its own `bounds` (or has a `model`) looks the same, since the
     renderer still colours a box by the archetype's name; one that leaned on
     the default box (`furniture`'s 1 m cube, a `wall`'s 10 x 4 m slab) draws
     nothing, and a `door` no longer gets its `audio_trigger`;
   - `flint validate` in a game with no constraints of its own now says `No
     constraints found in provided schema directories.` and exits 0, where
     the three room-and-door rules used to run on every game.
2. **`flint schema model`, `flint schema light`, `flint schema spline` and
   `flint schema spline_mesh` print their fields**, with each fallback in its
   description. The fields are the ones the engine already read; nothing a
   scene wrote before means something else now. The loader checks these
   components against the schemas and warns, as it does for every other
   component: a `light` whose `type` is not `directional`, `point` or `spot`
   (it lit nothing before, in silence), a `model` with no `asset`, a
   `spline_mesh` without `spline`, `width` and `height`, a number where a
   list belongs. Loading fills in three defaults on a `light` that leaves
   them out, the values the renderer already used: `type = "directional"`,
   `color = [1, 1, 1]`, `intensity = 1.0`. A script that reads
   `get_field(light, "light", "intensity")` now gets `1.0` rather than
   nothing.
3. **`flint_core::components::HEALTH` and `PLAYER` are gone.** No engine code
   read a `health` or `player` component; a player extension that used the
   constants writes the strings `"health"` and `"player"`.

**What to do.** Search your scenes and prefabs for the seven archetype names:

    grep -rnE 'archetype = "(furniture|wall|floor|ceiling|pillar|room|door)"' scenes prefabs

If there are none, nothing. If there are, either keep the vocabulary by
listing the layer between the engine's set and your own, in `flint.toml` (and
in `bundle.toml`, if it has a `schemas` key of its own):

    [project]
    schemas = ["engine/schemas", "engine/schemas/examples", "schemas"]

or, better for a game that only ever wanted a box, copy the one or two
archetype files you use from `engine/schemas/examples/archetypes/` into your
own `schemas/archetypes/` and leave the layer out. A command line that passes
`--schemas engine/schemas --schemas schemas` needs `--schemas
engine/schemas/examples` between them in the first case. To run one of the
engine's examples from inside a game, pass all three.

While you are in the scenes: nothing has ever read a
`[entities.x.directional_light]` or `[entities.x.point_light]` table, and a
scene whose only lights are spelled that way is lit by the engine's built-in
key and fill. `flint schema light` shows the real table: `[entities.x.light]`
with `type = "directional"`, and a `direction` that points *toward* the
light, so a sun written as the way its rays travel (`[-0.5, -0.6, -0.3]`)
becomes `[0.5, 0.6, 0.3]`. Twenty-seven of the engine's own example scenes
were written that way and now carry real lights, so they look different:
longer shadows, the lantern and fire lights lit. Nothing reads `cast_shadows`
on a `light` either (it is a `material` field).

### Viewer Ctrl+S and `flint validate --fix` keep the rest of the scene file (#677)
**What changed.** Both used to rewrite an existing scene from its entities
alone, which deleted `[camera]`, `[environment]`, `[post_process]`,
`[living]`, `[wooze]`, `[prefabs]`, the `[scene]` description and
`input_config`, and every comment; `--fix` also renamed the scene "scene" and
baked prefab entities into the file. Now:

- **Ctrl+S in `flint edit`** writes only the fields you edited, each in place
  with its comment. With nothing edited it writes nothing, so saving an
  untouched scene leaves it byte for byte as it was. A file with CRLF line
  endings keeps them.
- **`flint validate --fix`** writes only what its fixes changed
  (`SceneDocument::apply_world_diff`). A fix to an entity that comes from a
  prefab is printed as `not written` rather than baked in. `--output-diff` is
  the diff of the file.
- **`flint_scene::save_scene` refuses a path that already exists** and
  returns an error saying so. It is for new scenes only.

**What to do.** A scene that lost its tables to an earlier save needs them put
back by hand (git has them). Rust code calling `save_scene` on an existing
file: load a `SceneDocument` and call `patch_field`, `remove_field`,
`add_entity` or `apply_world_diff`, then `save`.

### `flint init` writes a real game project: manifest, lit scene, HUD, input config, agent guide (#707)
**What changed.** `flint init <name>` used to write a room and a door with no
light, camera, player or script, its own copies of the engine's `transform`,
`bounds` and `door` schemas (which shadowed the engine's), a `furniture`
archetype, and a `CLAUDE.md` naming folders it hadn't made. It now writes the
game-project pattern, from templates compiled into the binary:

- `flint.toml` with `[project] id`, `schemas`, `scene` and `input`, so none of
  the commands it prints needs `--schemas` and `flint play` needs no scene;
- `scenes/main.scene.toml`: lit (a `light` with a `type`), with a `[camera]`,
  a ground and something to see; `scripts/hud.rhai` on a `hud_controller`;
  `config/input.toml`; `bundle.toml`; `.gitignore`; `renders/`;
- an **empty** `schemas/components` and `schemas/archetypes`: no engine schema
  is copied into the game, and there is no `schemas/constraints`, `levels/`
  or `assets/` folder any more;
- `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`, which name only paths and commands that exist.

New flags: `--template walk|blank|2d` (default `walk`: a first-person player
facing a lit ring of stones), `--engine path|subtree|none`, `--engine-path
<dir>` and `--force`.

- `--engine path` (the default) names the Flint checkout the `flint` binary
  was built from, by absolute path, in `flint.toml` and in `bundle.toml`'s
  `[mount]`. The printed commands work at once; the path is right on that
  machine only.
- `--engine subtree` names `engine/schemas`, the pattern the games use.
  `flint init` runs no git and no network commands: it prints the `git
  subtree add`, and until that has run every command stops with `schema
  directory not found`.
- `--engine none` names only the game's `schemas/`. The scene validates and
  renders, engine archetypes resolve to nothing, and it does not offer
  `flint play`.

It refuses a directory that already holds files (it used to refuse any
existing directory). With `--force` it writes into one and keeps every file
already there. The "Next steps" no longer print the hidden `flint serve`
alias.

**What to do.** Nothing for an existing game: `flint init` only writes new
projects. A script that ran `flint init x` and then read `x/levels/demo.scene.toml`
or `x/schemas/components/*.toml` must read `x/scenes/main.scene.toml` instead,
and will find the game's `schemas/` empty. A game still carrying the old
scaffold's `schemas/components/transform.toml`, `bounds.toml` or `door.toml`
should delete them: they hide the engine's (the old `transform` has no
`shear` or `rotation_quat`).

## 2026-11-10

### `flint play` and `flint bundle` read `flint.toml`; saves, `load_ui`, `load_data` and the procgen cache use the project root (#706)
**What changed.** The last pieces of the project manifest (ADR 0114). Every
key of `flint.toml`'s `[project]` table now has a reader, and nothing in the
engine guesses the project root any more.

- **`flint play` and `flint-player` with no scene argument** open the
  manifest's `scene`. With no such key the error says what to pass and that
  `[project] scene` can name a default.
- **`flint bundle` and `flint export web`** take the bundle's name from
  `[project] id` and its start scene from `[project] scene` when `bundle.toml`
  doesn't say (`--name` / `--scene`, then `bundle.toml`, then `flint.toml`,
  then the folder's name and the only scene). `bundle.toml` is still the
  packing rules, and may now hold nothing else. `flint.toml` is packed with
  the game, and the player reads it from the bundle.
- **Paths written "from the project root"** are joined to the project's root,
  the folder with the `flint.toml` (or the `.flint/`, or the `schemas/` +
  `scenes/` layout), whichever scene is playing: `load_ui("ui/hud.ui.toml")`
  and a style sheet's `tokens`, `load_data("data/x.toml")`, a
  `music_session`'s `manifest` and `chart`, the save file
  `save/persist.toml`, the procgen disk cache `.flint/procgen-cache/` and the
  project-local input override `.flint/input.user.toml`. Each used to guess
  the root as "the scene folder's parent", in its own copy.
- A terrain's `splat_map` and `layer*_texture`, and the path
  `load_chunk(path)` takes, are found by the one search (from the scene's
  directory, then each directory above it), as the heightmap already was. The
  particle editor finds an effect's textures the same way, from the effect's
  folder. `flint bundle`'s reference check walks to the root and the engine
  too, so a nested scene's references pass where the runtime finds them.

**Nothing moves for a scene directly in `scenes/` or `levels/`**: the scene
folder's parent *is* the project root there, by every marker, and a game with
no marker at all keeps the old guess. Two layouts differ:

| Layout | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| a scene two or more folders deep, `scenes/act1/a.scene.toml` | `scenes/save/persist.toml`, `scenes/ui/...`, and a cache at `scenes/act1/.flint/procgen-cache/` | `save/persist.toml`, `ui/...`, `.flint/procgen-cache/` at the root: the same files a scene in `scenes/` uses |
| a `.flint/` folder beside the scenes (an old procgen cache, a shipped `input.user.toml`), which marks that folder as a root | the parent of the scenes' folder | the scenes' folder |

So a save no longer moves when a transition enters a deeper scene (the first
half of #726).

**For one release the old place is still read.** When a UI document, a data
file, a music session's manifest or the save file is not at the root and is
at the old guessed place, it is read from there, with a warning on stderr
naming both paths. The save is written at the root from then on; the old file
is left where it is. A `.flint/input.user.toml` beside the scene is still read
(and saved back to) when the root has none. The procgen cache is not carried
over: it regenerates once.

**What to do.** Nothing for a game whose scenes sit directly in `scenes/` or
`levels/`. If a warning names an old place, move the file to the path it
gives. To use the new defaults, give `flint.toml` a `scene` (and an `id`),
and drop `name` / `scene` / `schemas` from `bundle.toml` if they only repeated
them. A stray `.flint/` folder beside your scenes makes that folder a project
root of its own (the nearest marker wins, hiding a `flint.toml` above it):
delete it if it holds only an old `procgen-cache/`.

### Looks, palettes, glyph sets, fonts, paper and stroke images and user styles are found by the one search rule; the working directory is no longer searched (#706)
**What changed.** The last kinds join the engine's one asset search: beside
the scene, then beside each directory above it up to the project root, then
under `engine/` at the root. With them every kind of asset a scene names is
found the same way. What each looks for at a directory `D` of that walk:

| Kind (`flint asset where <kind>`) | Named by | Tried at each `D` |
|---|---|---|
| `looks` | `[post_process] look = "dusk"`, `--look dusk`, `set_look("dusk")` | `D/styles/dusk.look.toml`, then `D/dusk.look.toml` |
| `palettes` | a style's `palette = "dusk"`, `--ascii-palette` | `D/palettes/dusk.palette.toml`, then `D/dusk.palette.toml` |
| `glyph-sets` | a style's `font = "terminal"`, `--ascii-font`, `ui_text.font = "terminal"` | `D/fonts/terminal.glyphs.toml`, then `D/terminal.glyphs.toml` |
| `fonts` | `ui_text.font = "fonts/ui.font.toml"` | `D/fonts/ui.font.toml` (the path from `D`), then `D/fonts/<path>` |
| `font-faces` | the script HUD's fonts; a `fonts/fonts.toml` alias's `file` | every `.ttf` / `.otf` in `D/fonts/` |
| `papers` | `paper_image = "cold_press"` (pencil, watercolor) | `D/papers/cold_press.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, then the same in `D` |
| `strokes` | `stroke_image = "graphite"` (pencil) | `D/strokes/graphite.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, then the same in `D` |
| `styles` | user styles: `[post_process.styles.glow]` | every `D/styles/*.shader.toml` |

A name written as a path (`--look out/tuned.look.toml`, `palette =
"art/dusk.palette.toml"`, `paper_image = "scans/linen.jpg"`) is that path from
each `D`.

**Built-ins come after the search**, as before: the built-in palettes
(`cga`, `phosphor`, `amber`, ...) and looks (`graphite`, `matrix`, ...) are
used when the search finds no file of the name, and a file it finds wins. The
glyph set `builtin` is never searched for. `flint asset where palettes amber`
says when the answer is the built-in, and exits 0.

**Found now where nothing was found before** (no warning: nothing loads
differently, something loads that did not):

- **A scene more than one folder below the project root** finds the project's
  `styles/`, `palettes/`, `fonts/`, `papers/` and `strokes/`. Each of these
  searches stopped one level above the scene before, so a look or palette
  named from `scenes/act1/cave/` fell back to a built-in or a warning.
- **The engine subtree's files.** `engine/styles/*.look.toml`,
  `engine/palettes/`, `engine/fonts/` and `engine/styles/*.shader.toml` are
  searched last. The eight shipped looks are files in `engine/styles/`, so a
  game with an engine subtree now reads them from the subtree instead of the
  copies compiled into the binary. They are the same looks unless the subtree
  and the binary are different engine versions.
- **The script HUD's fonts are every `fonts/` the scene can see**, not the
  first one that exists. A `fonts/` beside a scene used to hide the project's
  `fonts/` whole; now it adds to it, hiding only files of the same name. The
  `fonts/fonts.toml` read is the nearest one. A `ui_text.font` naming a
  `.font.toml` was read from the scene's own folder only; now from each folder
  above it too.
- **A user style in a `styles/` two or more folders up**, and one in
  `engine/styles/`.

**The working directory is no longer searched.** Before, a look was also
looked for in `styles/` of the directory the command was run from, a palette
in its `palettes/`, a glyph set in its `fonts/` (in the ASCII style), a paper
or stroke image in it and its `papers/` / `strokes/`; and `--look
some/file.toml`, or a `.palette.toml` / `.glyphs.toml` path, was read as a
path from the working directory. Now the only places are the ones in the rule.
Run from the project root this changes nothing: the root is the last
directory of the walk, so `flint render levels/a.scene.toml --look
out/tuned.look.toml` from the root still finds `out/tuned.look.toml`. It
changes when a command is run from somewhere else, or the file is outside the
scene's project. With no scene at all (`flint edit model.glb --style ...`,
`--list-styles` with no scene), the working directory stands in for the
scene's folder, as before.

**User styles of one name.** With a `glow.shader.toml` in two `styles/`
folders, a scene gets the one nearest to it. Before, the first scene loaded in
a run decided `glow` for every later scene and the other file was skipped
with a warning (and `flint bundle` refused the game). Now each scene's
nearest takes the name when that scene loads, and a nearer file dropped in
while the scene is open takes over at the next reload poll.

**What to do.** Usually nothing. Where a pick changed, the engine says so for
one release, once per file, on stderr when the scene loads and as a `note` in
`flint asset where`:

```
look 'dusk': the search order changed (ADR 0114). Before, the engine loaded styles/dusk.look.toml; now it loads none: that file is outside the project. Move it into the project, or put a flint.toml at the folder that holds both
```

If you see it, move the file into the project (or run from, or put a
`flint.toml` at, the folder that holds both). A missing look, palette, glyph
set, font or image now lists every path tried in its warning. A font named
`Body.TTF` is still loaded; other extensions are matched as spelled
(`cotton.PNG` is not found by `paper_image = "cotton"`; name it `cotton.PNG`
in full).

**For a game that embeds the engine** (`PlayerApp`, `flint_render`): the
lookups take the scene's project now. `GlyphSetCache::new` and
`PaletteCache::new` take a `flint_core::project::AssetScope` (a project and a
